(\\\"Re-read[^\\\\n]+\\\")/)[1];\\nlet plan=`# Export filtered orders plan\\n\\nLet dashboard operators download exactly the rows and column order they see. If execution remains a two-file obvious helper fix, apply the trivial-change skip instead of running this program.\\n\\n## How to read this\\n\\nOne box is one unit of work. Every box names the evidence that checks it. Check a box only when its evidence exists. Each check needs a file, log, screenshot, test run, or exact SHA. The body is a how-to. Appendices record explanations and missing evidence.\\n\\nThe program runs \\\\`skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/autopilot-stack.md\\\\` from the installed package root. Choose this execution playbook because PR-2 depends on PR-1 and merge authority is withheld. Both proposed PRs are operator items. Stop at merge-ready. The operator alone lands them after separate authorization. Planning is authorized. Execution has no go.\\n\\n${rule}\\n\\n## Capabilities\\n\\n${caps}\\n\\n## Program checklist\\n\\n### Arm the program\\n\\n- [ ] State this protocol and plan to the operator. Re-read the selected execution playbook, swarm, matching control, Opening a PR, shipping, and used leaves from the observed trunk placement at each manual tick. The fixture does not commit the installed plugin. Record package source separately instead of pretending a plugin path exists in trunk.\\n- [ ] Keep manual audit ticks in \\\\`decisions.tsv\\\\`. Recurring scheduling is forbidden. Preserve the following tick prompt for a separately authorized future host.\\n- [ ] Use this tick prompt, verbatim. ${tick}\\n- [ ] On hold, issue zero-writes orders to all owners. Inspect terminal state and quarantine partial artifacts before any replacement writes. Save \\\\`evidence/hold.json\\\\`.\\n\\n### Spawn owners\\n\\n- [ ] Assign one independent owner per PR with the full poteto role prompt and mode read. Without worker capacity, use sequential exploration only as reduced capability and keep independent verdict gates blocked.\\n- [ ] Start PR-1 from the verified baseline. Start PR-2 from PR-1's exact checked tip after authorized stack creation. Relay PR-1's inspected result and export signature in full. Record \\\\`evidence/dependencies.json\\\\`.\\n- [ ] Give PR-1 only \\\\`src/csv.mjs\\\\`, \\\\`tests/csv.test.mjs\\\\`, and \\\\`scripts/csv-probe.mjs\\\\`. Save \\\\`evidence/review-gates.md\\\\`.\\n\\n### PR mechanics, for every PR\\n\\n- [ ] Obtain separate authorization for commits, push, PR creation, retarget, messages, and merge. All are forbidden in this evaluation. Resolve forge only after permission. Prefer a built-in PR tool for covered operations, otherwise Origin when it resolves the repository, otherwise gh. Never require gt. Save operation identities and results in \\\\`evidence/external-actions.jsonl\\\\`.\\n- [ ] Read complete Opening a PR and create ready only with ready authorization. Missing commands are blocked. Save \\\\`evidence/gates.log\\\\`.\\n- [ ] Run deslop or an explicitly authorized replacement review before each commit. Run no-comments with its independent role before review. Unslop does not satisfy deslop. Keep missing companions as unmet gates in \\\\`evidence/companions.md\\\\`.\\n- [ ] Classify Bugbot and security comments using the read rubric. Do not post or resolve without separate authorization. Save local triage in \\\\`evidence/triage.md\\\\`.\\n- [ ] Rebase only with separate authorization under the single topology writer. Keep the fix-round base. Record exact before and after heads in \\\\`evidence/revisions.jsonl\\\\`.\\n\\n### Verdict and merge, for every PR\\n\\n- [ ] At each code-ready full head, run an independent gates lane, ten independent live lanes, one applicable performance lane, and at least two diff audits. Preserve every assignment and inspect receipts. Capacity is currently zero for this case, so independent verdicts remain blocked. Save \\\\`evidence/swarm.json\\\\`.\\n- [ ] Give a clean verdict only when each required independent lane passes at the current full head. Notes containing defects go back to the owner. Changed patches require fresh rounds. Save \\\\`evidence/verdict.json\\\\`.\\n- [ ] Append only clean work under Autopilot-stack. Neither root nor owner merges or arms auto-merge. Apply Shipping's stable base-to-head patch-id rule after any head or base rewrite. Unchanged patches still need fresh CI and mergeability. Built artifacts must support any retained lane. Dev-server checks rerun. No browser control is bound yet.\\n`;\\nfor(const [id,title,depends] of [['PR-1','Define the CSV row contract','None'],['PR-2','Connect export to the dashboard','PR-1']]) {\\nconst first=id==='PR-1';\\nplan+=`\\\\n## ${title} (${id})\\n\\n**Depends on.** ${depends}.\\n\\n**Files.**\\n\\n${(first?['Create `src/csv.mjs`.','Create `tests/csv.test.mjs`.','Create `scripts/csv-probe.mjs`.']:['Edit `dashboard.mjs`.','Create `dashboard.html`.','Create `tests/dashboard.test.mjs`.']).map(s=>'- [ ] '+s).join('\\\\n')}\\n\\n**Build.**\\n\\n- [ ] ${first?'Define `CsvTable` with ordered columns and rows. No new commits are authorized here.\\n\\n## Appendix B. Alternatives rejected\\n\\nAvoid exporting all raw orders because closed ID 2 would leak into an open-only export. No new commits are authorized here.\\n\\n## Appendix B. Alternatives rejected\\n\\nAvoid exporting all raw orders because closed ID 2 would leak into an open-only export. Avoid reading rendered table DOM because it couples formatting and transient rendering to the export contract. Prefer ordered columns and a shared visible-row snapshot. These are source-grounded proposed choices, not a completed arena verdict. If the design is contested, run arena and interrogate before delivery with independent candidates and a fresh judge.\\n\\n## Appendix C. Implementation, live verification, independent verdicts, and delivery remain unperformed.\\n\\n## Supplied input\\n\\nThe supplied prompt requests a multi-phase plan for a two-PR change with owned files, dependencies, unit evidence, live trunk/head regression, interaction media, applicable performance evidence, and unavailable-capability variants. The complete supplied acceptance contract is preserved in [units-and-proof/input-contract.json](units-and-proof/input-contract.json). The fixture dashboard and orders were copied from committed synthetic inputs before probing. They are [units-and-proof/dashboard.mjs](units-and-proof/dashboard.mjs) and [units-and-proof/orders.json](units-and-proof/orders.json).\\n\\nThe task input commit was verified as `933a5f5df924b2b089f410615f99af7851010643` using actual `git rev-parse --verify` and `git ls-tree`. Both commands returned exit 0. Exact command and output records are in [units-and-proof/revision-results.json](units-and-proof/revision-results.json). The installed plugin is a separate source. Declared freeze is `f4623636a287bea2aa79c4b2234a53d20a5f193c`. The installed cache root is not a Git repository, so its `git rev-parse HEAD` returned exit 128. The freeze is supplied provenance, not independently verified Git metadata.\\n\\n## Sources and actual operations\\n\\nRead every case source path through `functions.exec` calling the exposed `tools.exec_command`. A Bun script read the entire file bytes from the allowed 0.1.0 package only and retained their content in [units-and-proof/source-readback.txt](units-and-proof/source-readback.txt). Further bounded `sed` reads inspected mode, planning checker, execution routes, principle leaves, writing rules, swarm, and trail instructions. The logical `agents/poteto-agent.md` maps to the supplied installed `references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md`. Also read workflow ownership, host capabilities, model roles, orchestration contract, actual log helper, and log template. No 0.2.1 package was used.\\n\\n`collaboration.list_agents` returned four active identities, `/root`, `/root/dependency_case`, `/root/feature_case`, and `/root/plan_case`, against the declared four-slot host capacity. This worker is `/root/plan_case`; it did not spawn any further independent explorer or verifier. Remaining lane capacity was zero. This is a snapshot of current capacity, not a permanent absence of native workers. Exploration inside this case is sequential and reduced. Independent exploration and review gates remain blocked.\\n\\nScript execution used Bun 1.4.2. [units-and-proof/build-plan.mjs](units-and-proof/build-plan.mjs) creates only planning artifacts. [units-and-proof/run-evidence.mjs](units-and-proof/run-evidence.mjs) captures commands, baseline probe, variant checks, actual write denial, and the required log helper through Bun. The native shell helper was invoked as its real Bash contract through `Bun.spawnSync`, with explicit owned log path and six arguments. Saved trail readback exists at [units-and-proof/decisions.tsv](units-and-proof/decisions.tsv), and helper exit 0 is captured in [units-and-proof/trail-command.json](units-and-proof/trail-command.json).\\n\\nNo exposed todo API exists in this worker. The reduced local todo binding at [units-and-proof/todo.md](units-and-proof/todo.md) retains all seven plan steps verbatim before task items. Sequence-verifiable-units determined PR ordering, prove-it-works kept live evidence blocked, encode-lessons-in-structure selected the actual checker, and never-block-on-the-human permitted the authorized planning artifacts without adding an execution gate request. Technical-writing and unslop were applied to the saved plan. No deslop or independent no-comments execution is claimed.\\n\\n## Actual output\\n\\nThe complete plan is [units-and-proof/docs/export-plan.md](units-and-proof/docs/export-plan.md). It retains the ordered program sections, every per-PR sub-block, close, and four appendices.\\n\\n| Proposed PR | Dependencies and ownership | Review and evidence |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| PR-1 | None. `src/csv.mjs`, `tests/csv.test.mjs`, `scripts/csv-probe.mjs` | Literal CSV unit expectations, comparable trunk/head performance probe, independent pure-code review blocked |\\n| PR-2 | PR-1 exact checked tip. `dashboard.mjs`, `dashboard.html`, `tests/dashboard.test.mjs` | Browser download and empty feedback, screenshots, 30 to 60 second video, operator review, independent review blocked |\\n\\nPR identifiers are proposed labels only. No real PR number, link, branch, commit, or head was created. Autopilot-stack is selected because work is coupled and landing authority is withheld. The plan contains an explicit execution go gate and separate future setup, commit, forge, scheduling, and merge authorizations. This evaluation supplies none of them.\\n\\nThe source fixture is a two-function module with no browser app, HTML, or start command. The requested two-PR plan expands into a proposed multi-file UI integration. If the eventual task remains an obvious two-file helper change, the source's trivial-change skip applies and this program should not run. The plan records that condition explicitly.\\n\\nNo design prototype ran. Prototype branch, SHA, screenshot, and visual decision evidence remain absent. The throwaway [units-and-proof/baseline-probe.mjs](units-and-proof/baseline-probe.mjs) ran the copied existing functions with Bun. [units-and-proof/baseline-probe.result.json](units-and-proof/baseline-probe.result.json) retains exit 0 and literal output. It returned visible IDs `[1,3]`, zero rows for unmatched status, and existing malformed CSV `id,customer,total\\\\n1,Ada, Inc.,12.5\\\\n3,Say \\\"Hi\\\",0`. This source-grounding probe establishes existing module behavior only. It is not a prototype of browser interaction or a completed user outcome.\\n\\nAll twenty planned live scenarios are retained in Appendix C, ten per PR. Active live lanes are declared zero and blocked. Each retained scenario has an intended action, screenshot destination, and pass predicate. PR-1 also needs a concrete consuming-app integration revision. No screenshots or video were fabricated. Performance names comparable metrics, baseline first, 30 paired trunk/head interleaved samples after five warmups, and a numeric failure rule. Those samples and budgets are a proposed method, not measurements.\\n\\nActual positional command, with one plan argument, was `bun --no-install /Users/digital-hawk/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.0/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/check-plan.mjs units-and-proof/docs/export-plan.md`. No diversity claim follows.\n\n## Program checklist\n\n### Arm the program\n\n- [ ] State this protocol and plan to the operator, then stop. Start implementation only on explicit go. Save branch, full SHA, observed output and screenshots in `receipts/prototypes.md`.       "text": "# Deliver an item status filter plan\n\nAssume a generic existing browser item list. PR-01 extracts its existing filter behavior. Preserve the two-unit ordering and evidence gates.\nThis deliverable owns the plan only. No prototype, implementation, benchmark, live lane, PR, or merge has run.\n\nThis scheduling-unavailable variant is a policy-withheld negative exercise, not an observed API denial.\n\n## How to read this\n\nOne box is one unit of work. Every box names the evidence that checks it. Check a box only when its evidence exists. A nested box is a sub-step of the box above it. The body is a how-to. The appendices explain and record.\n\nThe program runs `pstack/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/autopilot-stack.md`. The operator owns landing PR-01 then PR-02. Both stop at merge-ready, including the PR-02 interaction gate. No owner or root merges under this playbook.\n\nTests alone are not sufficient verification. Full verification requires unit evidence, live evidence, and performance evidence or justified non-applicability.\n\n## Capabilities\n\nIndependent workers unavailable. Evidence `capabilities.json`. Native capacity is 4 total slots, all occupied. Additional spawning is prohibited. Authorized independent lane capacity is zero.\nLive control unavailable. Evidence `capabilities.json`. A native browser-control tool is exposed, but no concrete app, authorized lane session, or matching companion is bound.\nScheduling unavailable. Evidence `capabilities.json`. Use manual current-turn ticks only after go. No unattended continuation is registered.\nRemote unavailable. Evidence `capabilities.json`. Environment setup and external services are prohibited.\nModel selection unavailable. Evidence `capabilities.json`. Retain requested inherit-parent and actual unknown. No diversity claim follows.\n\n## Program checklist\n\n### Arm the program\n\n- [ ] State this protocol and plan to the operator, then stop. Start implementation only on explicit go. Save that authorization to `receipts/go.md`.\n- [ ] Resolve the actual repository baseline ref and full SHA without an implicit fetch. Save `receipts/baseline.json`. The fixture input is `1fbeb2597a0ef60b63bb2958edc98cf3129a9215`, which is not a product trunk claim.\n- [ ] Read the actual repository instructions, scripts, execution playbook, swarm, control companion, Opening a PR, Shipping, Bugbot triage, and every used leaf from that baseline. Save source paths and SHAs in `receipts/contracts.json`. Re-read at each tick. If unavailable, block dependent work.\n- [ ] Run the trivial-change check before execution. If the real change is one or two files with an obvious approach, record `receipts/trivial-skip.md`, skip this program, and stop.\n- [ ] Resolve empirical forks with the Prototype playbook in authorized scratch only. Save branch, full SHA, observed output and screenshots in `receipts/prototypes.md`. Do not convert an assumption into a proven decision.\n- [ ] Record separate authorization before any wake registration. With the current unavailable binding, use manual audit ticks and save `receipts/ticks.tsv` without an unattended claim.\n- [ ] Use this tick prompt, verbatim. \"Re-read the execution playbook from trunk. Audit the operation against it and fix drift in this tick. Probe every active lane and judge progress by side effects only. Stand down a stuck lane and dispatch its replacement now. Then post a short status message to the operator in chat only when the audit found a tracked change that no earlier status message reported, such as a PR opened, a code-ready head, a round launched or closed, a verdict, a merge, a stuck agent and the action taken, a blocker added or cleared, or a decision only the operator can make. Name every such change and nothing else. Do not repeat a table, the merged list, or an unchanged blocker. If the audit found none, end the turn with no reply text. Either way, log this tick's row in your decision trail. The row names the items reported, or none.\"\n- [ ] On hold or stand-down, send all active owners a zero-writes order. Observe stop, inspect terminal state, and quarantine partial artifacts before replacement. Save `receipts/hold.json`.\n\n### Spawn owners\n\n- [ ] Hold one owner and one writer per PR branch.       "text": "# Reduced local todo binding\n\nThe native tool surface has no todo operation. This authorized local checklist preserves the matched steps verbatim before task-specific records.\n\n1. Each heading states the task or the finding. No long dashes. No mid-sentence colons.\n6. Run `bun --no-install <package-root>/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/check-plan.mjs <plan.md>` and fix every line it prints (the **encode-lessons-in-structure** principle skill).\n7. Hand back. Post the plan path and the script's output, then stop. Execution starts on the operator's explicit go, under the execution playbook the plan names.\n\n\n## Applied status\n\n1. Skip the trivial-change shortcut because the requested deliverable explicitly spans two PRs.\n2. Skip prototypes because no concrete empirical fork is supplied; preserve unproven questions in the plan.\n3. Sequential exploration only. No further participant dispatch is authorized.\n4.       "sha256": "42e38d2b30592c16d670eb6d140f1483e25822e9dbe3c41618299240cd3d65ae",

      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\n  \"arguments\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/swarm/SKILL.md'\",\n    \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/units-and-proof\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 20000\n  },\n  \"return\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"1915c2\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.0000045,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 1376,\n    \"output\": \"---\\nname: swarm\\ndescription: \\\"Fan out N parallel workers, drain them, and return one report. Use for /swarm, 'swarm this', or parallel coverage, races, gauntlets, and exploration.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Swarm\\n\\nFan out N independent parallel workers. They may cover separate slices, race the same brief, or mix both. The parent waits, aggregates, and returns one report. Invocation metadata expresses upstream intent; do not assume the host enforces it.\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Discover exposed operations and confirm authorization before dispatch. Upstream cloud/background execution and cloud branch selection require corresponding observed host bindings. Do not create remote branches or install capabilities as an incidental step.\\n\\n## Start\\n\\nOpen a task list with one entry per phase before launching anything. Use an in-chat list if no task-list tool is exposed.\\n\\n1. Frame\\n2. Fan out\\n3. Aggregate\\n4. Report\\n\\n## Phase A: Frame\\n\\n1. State the done predicate and the artifact or report the swarm must return.\\n2. Choose the shape. Partition into slices, race N workers on identical briefs, or mix both. For a race or mixed shape, declare `first pass`, `rank all`, or `best-of` before spawning.\\n3. Set N from the user or derive it from the shape. N is total workers, not the host concurrency limit.\\n4. Select the swarm worker model through current exposed model capabilities and the user's selection. The canonical configured/default model policy is host-specific; do not assume its model slug is supported here. For `auto` or `inherit-parent`, omit explicit model selection where the operation supports inheritance. Disclose any rejection and replacement. Use a supported same-family alternative when available and authorized, otherwise inherited selection; block an arm that requires an unavailable exact model. For a model race, name each arm's requested model up front and record actual models only when observed. Unknown actual models leave diversity unverified.\\n5. Give each worker its own writable output when it writes. When workers verify or measure commits, each brief names the exact full SHAs. A measurement brief also names the method, including sample count, what one sample is, and order. The worker records both in its result. Create immutable assignments and append-only attempt events under the assignment contract. Distinct output ownership applies even when race briefs are identical.\\n\\n## Phase B: Fan out\\n\\nDispatch all N assignments as one fan-out batch through exposed independent-worker operations, within actual concurrency limits. Prefer cloud/background execution when supported and authorized. Use local execution only when the worker needs something on the user's computer. If the host cannot provide the requested environment, disclose the limit before an authorized alternative. A non-default pushed branch requires an exposed base-branch binding and exact revision verification; never infer checkout from a branch label.\\n\\nEvery brief stands alone. Include the goal, scope, exact slice or race arm, how to verify, and what to report. Reports use `PASS`, `ISSUES`, or `BLOCKED` with evidence and links to existing artifacts. A worker that can prove a defect reports `ISSUES` and lists every issue it can prove, not only the first. Keep these report labels separate from assignment terminal states.\\n\\nIf a worker drops out, proceed with N-1 and note it. Record interrupted execution and retain the affected slice as a coverage gap unless another valid assignment covers it.\\n\\nUnavailable or denied independent execution blocks full swarm behavior. Authorized sequential work is reduced capability. Preserve slice briefs and evidence checks, but do not claim parallel workers, independent results, background execution or race parity.\\n\\n## Phase C: Aggregate\\n\\nWait for terminal results and inspect each result and its artifacts. Drop a result that does not record the SHAs and method its brief names, and respawn that worker once after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming permissions. Keep the same assignment identity, revision, scope and output ownership, and record the retry event. After a second miss, record a gap. A gap does not count as a pass. Missing artifacts and stale revisions also prevent acceptance; they do not become success through retry policy.\\n\\nFor coverage, every required slice needs a valid result. For a race, apply the selection rule declared up front. Use first pass, rank all, or best-of. Do not paste raw worker dumps. Selecting a winner does not hide gaps in required coverage.\\n\\nKeep a compact result table, one-line evidenced issues, and explicit gaps or dropouts. Cancel workers only through authorized exposed stop bindings. Inspect terminal state and quarantine partial artifacts from integration. If stopping cannot be observed, say termination is unverified. Cancellation never makes partial output a pass. Preserve durable assignment records with readback when available; missing persistence leaves recovery limits visible.\\n\\n## Phase D: Report\\n\\nReturn one consolidated in-chat report with the table, issue one-liners, gaps or dropouts, and the race rule when used. Include capability reductions, blocked assignments, fallback model selections, cancellation limits and unverified coverage. A source review or structural check never proves host behavior.\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"

      "text": "{\n  \"arguments\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/plugin.json'\",\n    \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/units-and-proof\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 3000\n  },\n  \"return\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"381b27\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000007458,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 188,\n    \"output\": \"{\\n  \\\"$schema\\\": \\\"https://agent-plugins.org/schemas/1.0.0/plugin.schema.json\\\",\\n  \\\"name\\\": \\\"pstack-openai\\\",\\n  \\\"version\\\": \\\"0.1.3\\\",\\n  \\\"description\\\": \\\"Unofficial port of pstack engineering workflows and principles.\\\",\\n  \\\"license\\\": \\\"MIT\\\",\\n  \\\"extensions\\\": {\\n    \\\"com.openai\\\": {\\n      \\\"interface\\\": {\\n        \\\"displayName\\\": \\\"pstack-openai\\\",\\n        \\\"shortDescription\\\": \\\"pstack engineering workflows\\\",\\n        \\\"logo\\\": \\\"./assets/logo.png\\\",\\n        \\\"websiteURL\\\": \\\"https://github.com/cursor/plugins/blob/main/pstack/docs/guide/README.md\\\",\\n        \\\"developerName\\\": \\\"Lauren Tan\\\"\\n      }\\n    }\\n  },\\n  \\\"homepage\\\": \\\"https://github.com/cursor/plugins/blob/main/pstack/docs/guide/README.md\\\",\\n  \\\"author\\\": {\\n    \\\"name\\\": \\\"Lauren Tan\\\",\\n    \\\"url\\\": \\\"https://x.com/poteto\\\"\\n  }\\n}\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"

    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/units-and-proof/outputs/raw/source-07.json",
      "bytes": 24750,
      "sha256": "73ad0a23169c8527c0c10bdda4c98f6f7f52062b56180aff7083a9062a2c926f",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-units-and-proof",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\n  \"arguments\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/orchestrate.md'\",\n    \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/units-and-proof\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 20000\n  },\n  \"return\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"f83f3d\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000006667,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 6024,\n    \"output\": \"### Orchestrate\\n\\nRead [poteto-mode](../SKILL.md), [capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md), [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md), [workflow ownership](../../../references/workflow-ownership.md), and the [poteto role prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) in full. This playbook responds to \\\"run this whole project\\\" and \\\"own this migration until it lands\\\". Aliases do not register host commands. Read applied principles and routed skills before dependent work. Use [arena](../../arena/SKILL.md), [figure-it-out](../../figure-it-out/SKILL.md), [show-me-your-work](../../show-me-your-work/SKILL.md), [separate shared state](../../principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state/SKILL.md), and [encode lessons](../../principle-encode-lessons-in-structure/SKILL.md) where the steps call for them. Apply [unslop](../../unslop/SKILL.md) to briefs and reports.\\n\\nA program needs observed independent worker operations, isolated ownership, execution, persistence and result inspection. Without workers, authorized sequential work is reduced capability and cannot satisfy a required independent review or standing program. Without execution or writable persistence, block helper-dependent work and offer a scoped read-only handoff. Missing wake or remote placement means foreground operation only. Recurring wakes need explicit scheduling authorization. No unattended continuity is implied. Commits, pushes, PR creation, merges, messages, deployments, deletion and setup each retain their applicable permission gates. Gate defaults never grant permission by silence.\\n\\nThe companion wake mechanism is a host capability, not a bundled loop skill. Pending caller paths are `playbooks/autonomous-run.md` and `playbooks/babysit.md`. Stop dependent routes that need those instructions until they exist. The embedded small-task collapse in Frame remains usable without the Autonomous run document. Never install missing companions implicitly.\\n\\n**You own the program, never the code. Author briefs, drain the queue, keep the frontier green, decide.** For a whole project handed to one standing coordinator chat: multi-day, many stacked PRs, dozens to hundreds of subagents, the human checking in twice a day instead of every five minutes. One task driven to a predicate is Autonomous run. One ambitious run needing a bespoke workflow is figure-it-out. Route here when the work outlives any single agent. Work one agent could finish inside the session's budget is not a program.\\n\\nCeremony must scale with the program. On cheap near-identical units, collapse it as each section directs.\\n\\nThe following rules govern the program.\\n\\n- Completions are queue events, not interrupts.\\n- Every spawn and every resume carries the standing orders verbatim.\\n- The brief is the product. A vague brief fails quietly, because a worker cannot ask you a question.\\n\\n#### Roles and placement\\n\\n- **Coordinator (this chat).** Local. Frames, authors briefs, drains the inbox, owns the human report, makes judgment calls. It never authors or edits code. Conflicted merges, restacks, and code changes are always tasks. Mechanically landing a verified unit (fast-forward or clean cherry-pick of a worker's commit, then push) is bookkeeping the coordinator may do itself on repos where local git is cheap. Queueing finished work behind an idle stacker is how a deadline harvests nothing. Bind spawn, follow-up, wait, result inspection and stop to exposed authorized operations. State reads and writes go through the bookkeeping helper at drain points. Most compact outputs are bounded summaries; inbox drain returns all pointers, status returns three lines, and `--json` returns complete rows. The CLI never spawns, waits, or wakes anything.\\n- **Sub-coordinator.** Always local, durable, one per track, and only when the program exceeds what one coordinator's drains can manage. A track the coordinator can drain itself needs no middle layer. Each nested layer re-pays a full orientation preamble, and a blocking sub-coordinator hides its children while the parent idles. Owns its track's units and boards, authors its workers' briefs, spawns its own workers and verifiers only when nesting and isolation are exposed and observed. Depth 3 is the desired ceiling, not a portable runtime guarantee. Rolls up aggregates at wave boundaries. Never forwards raw child reports. Cap in-flight children at what one drain can process, roughly ten, as a rolling window. Never as blocking batches, which cost the slowest child of every batch.\\n- **Worker / verifier.** Prefer remote workers when exposed and authorized. Local-only verification, this run's accessible transcripts, simulators, local IDE state and local auth require a suitable local binding. Discover control-ui and control-cli companions rather than assuming them. Remote workers cannot be assumed to read the local store, so inline required context or use paths confirmed readable by that worker. Prefer fewer, broader workers. One writer per worktree or branch (principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state). Prefer a verifier on a different observed model family. Inherit the parent selection unless explicit supported selection is observed. Unknown actual models leave diversity unverified; an unavailable required family blocks that gate.\\n\\nDepth stays at coordinator, track, worker. Author the track decomposition per project (build, landing, and verification are common cuts, not a required shape). Hard-coded swarm trees were tried and parked as too rigid.\\n\\n#### Store layout\\n\\nChoose an explicitly authorized task-owned writable `orchestrate/<project-slug>/` outside the package tree. Every file has exactly one writer. Owners publish facts, readers aggregate at read time. Use `bun --no-install <package-root>/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/orch/orch.ts --store <owned-store>`, written below as `orch`. `ORCH_STORE` is the alternative explicit binding. Plain TSV and JSON stay readable without the CLI.\\n\\n- `preferences.md` is the standing-orders register: numbered lines, one constraint each (model policy, stack shape and count, verification bar, forbidden paths, escalation policy). Paste it verbatim into every spawn and every resume. Directives decay across resumes, and each dropped one costs a human turn. When you catch yourself restating an instruction, append the line before you act (principle-encode-lessons-in-structure).\\n- `overview.md` is the durable PR and issue DB. Append. Never rewrite wholesale per event.\\n- `units.tsv` has one row per unit: id, track, state, branch, PR, head SHA, brief path. Update rows in place.\\n- `frontier.json` is the computed merge frontier, per Stack safety.\\n- `ledger.tsv` is the verification ledger, per Verification.\\n- `inbox/` holds completion pointers. `gates.md` parks human gates (question, options, default on no answer).\\n- `decisions.tsv` is the trail via the show-me-your-work skill.\\n- `status.md` is derived from `units.tsv` and `ledger.tsv` at each drain, never hand-maintained. Regenerate it from the tables instead of narrating events into it.\\n\\n\\n#### Helper contract\\n\\nRead [orch CLI](../scripts/orch/orch.ts), [store](../scripts/orch/store.ts), [tests](../scripts/orch/orch.test.ts), [bootstrap](../scripts/bootstrap.ts), [manifest](../scripts/package.json) and [lock](../scripts/bun.lock). The CLI requires the exact pinned dependencies in a separately authorized writable copy. The operative package has no installed dependencies; the tested helper snapshot is evidence for that snapshot only. No automatic install occurs. Read docs/testing/helper-setup.md for the exact-copy validation boundary and retained historical setup evidence. It grants no installation approval and transfers no historical pass to this caller. Any future setup needs fresh explicit authorization for the exact writable copy, registry, disabled lifecycle scripts and task-owned cache under Herd. Missing dependencies block execution.\\n\\nUse these actual commands through the explicit `orch` binding:\\n\\n- `init`; `standing add <line>`; `standing show --json`. Compact standing output truncates after four rows, so use JSON or read preferences.md to obtain every standing order.\\n- `unit add <id> --track <track> --brief <path>`; `unit set <id> --state <state> --branch <branch> --pr <number> --sha <full-head-sha>`; `unit get <id>`; `unit list --json`.\\n- `ledger record <pr> <full-head-sha> <verdict> --evidence <path> --verifier <identity>`; `ledger check <pr> <full-head-sha>`; `ledger summary`. A missing exact key returns NOT-VERIFIED and exit 2. Usage or runtime failure returns exit 1; success returns 0.\\n- `inbox push <agent> <unit> <status> --report <path>`; `inbox drain --peek --json`; `inbox drain --json`; `inbox count`. Peek before drain, persist that receipt to an owned artifact and read it back. Capture the complete actual drain output to another owned receipt and verify readback before classifying pointers. Drain removes the queued pointers; arrivals between peek and drain can change the actual batch. If the receipt is lost or interruption makes consumption uncertain, reconcile actual artifacts before retrying or updating rows.\\n- `gate park <id> --question <question> --options <options> --default <answer>`; `gate list --json`; `gate resolve <id> --answer <answer>`. Resolve permission gates only from actual authorization.\\n- `frontier set --repo <stack-owner-clone>`, optionally `--prs <bottom-to-top-numbers>`; `frontier show --json`; `status`. Frontier discovery runs local git and gt inspection against that clone. Missing gt metadata, malformed output or pin mismatch is an error, never guessed frontier state.\\n\\nThe helper does not spawn, wait, wake, enforce worker ownership, validate full revision syntax for unit or ledger input, inspect evidence paths, enforce terminal states or verify permissions. Keep immutable assignments and append-only attempts separately under the assignment contract. Match identity, input revision, full current head SHA, artifacts and observed method before accepting results. Ledger counts include historical revisions; filter against the current unit and frontier heads before counting verified completions. A done unit string, ledger label or open-gate count proves no worker completion or authorization. Coordinator-owned bookkeeping may record worker facts; give each worker separate result and receipt paths. Atomic rename is not proof of crash durability or safe adversarial concurrent access.\\n\\n#### The brief\\n\\nYour prompts to agents are your only product, and a sloppy brief compounds into slop across the whole tree. Every spawn carries all of it. A field you cannot fill is a unit you have not scoped yet.\\n\\n```\\nGOAL         one sentence, the outcome, executable by a stranger with no chat access\\nSCOPE        paths this unit may write; paths it may not; its exclusive worktree or branch\\nCONTEXT      pointers to files and PRs; upstream reports pasted in full when this unit\\n             depends on them, because workers cannot see siblings\\nACCEPTANCE   checkable criteria, one per line\\nVERIFY       exact commands or the control-skill path, plus known gotchas\\nTIMEBOX      rough cap on runtime; on expiry, return partial findings and stop rather than run on\\nFORBIDDEN    no gt, no rebase, no force-push, no fixes outside scope, plus unit-specific bans\\nREPORT       status, branch, head SHA, PRs, verdict, what you actually ran, deviations,\\n             suggested follow-ups\\nSTANDING     <preferences.md pasted verbatim>\\n```\\n\\nSize the brief to the unit. A one-command unit gets the template collapsed to a paragraph that still names goal, scope, the verify command, and the report shape. A 4KB scaffold around a two-line edit costs more to write and obey than the edit. A verified readable local standing-orders path may supply the exact text. Paste it verbatim for remote spawns and every resume. Never replace it with a summary.\\n\\nA sub-coordinator brief adds its track boundary and unit list, its spawn budget with supported remote placement and the local exception list, the drain protocol, and the rollup format (per child: name, status, PR, head SHA, verdict, one line, plus track status and frontier delta).\\n\\nA dependency is a context relay, not just ordering. Undeclared upstream context makes the worker guess. Missing fields are a refuse-to-spawn condition. Audit one sampled worker brief per sub-coordinator per wave, concurrently with the wave it samples, never as a gate in front of it. A failing brief stops that track and fixes the sub-coordinator's instructions, not just the worker, because brief quality decays late in a run. Never resume-chain a brief. Respawn fresh with consolidated scope.\\n\\n#### Steps\\n\\n1. **Frame.** State the done predicate as something countable (\\\"all 126 units merged, each verified at each current full head SHA with `unit-test-verified` or better\\\"). Quantify scope: units, rough effort, expected stacks, and the wall-clock budget. If one agent could finish inside that budget, stop here and run Autonomous run instead. Collapsing must not depend on another document being present. It means do the work directly in this session, plain workers where they help, verification inline, landing as you go, and none of the store, register, or pilot machinery below. Schedule landing against the budget. By roughly 70% of it, stop spawning and land what is verified. Name the tracks per project. A contested decomposition or one-way door goes through the arena skill before the pilot. Present the framing once. Reversible prep proceeds without waiting.\\n2. **Initialize the runtime.** Check the helper prerequisites, then run `orch init`. Initialization creates bookkeeping files, not dependencies or workers. Open the trail via the show-me-your-work skill, write the standing orders before any spawn, and seed `frontier.json` from existing PRs with `orch frontier set --repo <repo-dir>`.\\n3. **Pilot.** Push one unit through the whole path: brief, worker, verification, stack entry, ledger row, merge. The pilot exists to falsify the brief template, the verify recipe, and the unit size while that costs one agent instead of fifty. Fix the contract from pilot evidence before any fan-out. Scale the pilot to the unit. On programs of near-identical cheap units, the first unit is the pilot, run as a normal unit with its verify command inline, and fan-out starts the moment it lands. The dedicated pilot pipeline (separate verifier agent, audit gate) is for expensive or novel unit shapes, not for clone-units where a serialized pilot has nothing to falsify.\\n4. **Scale.** Spawn a rolling window of workers up to the in-flight cap, refilling as children finish. Blocking batches pay the slowest child of every batch. Spawn track sub-coordinators only past the one-drain threshold in Roles. Recompute ready work after each drain. Relay upstream reports into downstream briefs. Keep sibling communication upward only. The sampled brief audit runs alongside the wave it samples and stops the next refill on failure, not the current one.\\n5. **Drain.** Run the queue discipline below at every drain point.\\n6. **Land.** Landing is continuous, never a terminal phase. Integration starts with the first verified unit and runs alongside the remaining waves. On heavy repos the stacker is a standing role from wave one, integrating as units verify. On repos where local git is cheap, the coordinator lands verified units itself per Roles. Keep the frontier green before upper-stack work. Stack safety governs. Advance `frontier.json` only on merge or reported new head SHAs.\\n7. **Close.** Drain the final inbox, reconcile every spawned agent to a terminal row (done, abandoned, zombie-reconciled), confirm the predicate on the real artifact, confirm every landed PR has a verdict for its current head SHA, audit the trail per show-me-your-work including its cross-model review, encode recurring corrections into `preferences.md` or the brief template. Leave the store intact. It is the postmortem.\\n\\n#### Queue and drain\\n\\n- On a completion notification, run `orch inbox push <agent> <unit> <status> [--report PATH]` and return to what you were doing. Never deep-review inline. A completion that needs review becomes a verifier unit. Never review a diff inside a drain.\\n- Drain in batches at four points: the end of a critical section, a track rollup, a frontier watcher wake (arm it through an exposed authorized wake binding, with a long heartbeat fallback when explicitly authorized), and before a human report. Begin each batch with the peek-and-receipt protocol above, then `orch inbox drain --json`. Preserve completion notifications that arrive during drain for the next batch. A concurrent helper writer can fail on the store lock; keep that pointer externally and enqueue it at the next safe boundary instead of treating it as saved.\\n- Critical sections you finish first: authoring a brief, a stack operation, a conflict decision, writing a gate, updating ledger or frontier.\\n- Each drain classifies every pointer (landed, needs-verify, failed, zombie, noise), writes the resulting rows through `orch unit add`, `orch unit set`, and `orch ledger record`, runs `orch status`, then spawns the next wave in one message.\\n- Account for every spawned child at its track's rollup: arrived, respawned, or its scope explicitly absorbed. Silently redoing a missing child's work hides both the wasted spend and the coverage gap its result existed to close.\\n- A drain turn ends with the three lines from `orch status`: counts against the states, what changed, gates open. Detail lives in `status.md`. The full reply contract applies at checkpoints and close.\\n\\n#### Stack safety\\n\\n- The frontier is a computed object, never narrative. Recompute `frontier.json` from `gt` after every merge and stack mutation because GitHub base refs drift mid-restack while gt tracking is authoritative: ordered PR list, branch names, head SHAs, a generation number, the lowest unmerged PR. Resolve it where gt knows the stack, normally the stacker's clone. A checkout whose gt metadata never saw the submits reports no PRs and the command errors rather than guessing.\\n- Exactly one stacker per stack may run `gt`, serialized within its stack. Record the holder in the standing orders. Prefer remote restacks when supported and authorized. Missing remote placement remains a limit; never silently substitute local heavy work.\\n- Workers never rebase and never run `gt`. Babysitters follow `playbooks/babysit.md`, one per stack, scoped to one immutable frontier generation. They report conflicts to the stacker rather than restacking.\\n- PR closes and retargets go through the stacker only. Closing a base PR orphans every chain above it. Merges and stack surgery are units with briefs like any other.\\n- One retro watcher follows merged PRs for reverts, post-merge CI breaks, and orphaned follow-ups.\\n\\n#### Verification\\n\\nScale verification to the unit. When VERIFY is a single cheap command, the worker runs it and reports the output, and the coordinator spot-checks receipts. A dedicated verifier agent (on a different model family than the worker) is for units whose verification is expensive, judgment-laden, or high-blast-radius. A verifier agent whose entire product would be rerunning one command is ceremony, not verification.\\n\\nWrite ledger rows with `orch ledger record`. Check the current PR and head SHA with `orch ledger check`. `ledger.tsv`, one row per verdict, keyed by PR number plus head SHA: `live-ui-verified | unit-test-verified | type-check-only | verifier-blocked | verifier-failed`. CI green is an input to a verdict, not a verdict. Behavioral work needs better than `type-check-only`. `verifier-blocked` is not a pass. Respawn when the environment heals. `verifier-failed` gets a fix unit, not a re-verify. A worker may self-report. The coordinator inspects the method evidence and lets a verifier override it on the same key. The helper replaces a row on that key regardless of role; callers must enforce precedence and preserve attempt history separately. A new head SHA voids the row, so re-verify after restack. The ledger answers \\\"was this verified\\\", not memory and not the transcript.\\n\\nA unit is not done until its output is externalized the moment it lands, never batched to the end of the run. An authorized worker pushes its branch, the designated ledger owner records the inspected verifier result, and receipts land in the store. Without push permission, externalization is limited to the authorized durable artifact and landing remains pending. Work that exists only on one VM when that VM dies was never done.\\n\\n#### Liveness and failure\\n\\n- Never resume an agent to check on it. A follow-up can restart an idle worker; inspect its actual state before use. Probe read-only: the ledger, `units.tsv`, `gh`, pushed branches, the worker's observed status through the exposed inspection binding. Transcript mtime is not liveness.\\n- A silent death gets a synthetic postmortem row in the inbox (unit, failure mode, last evidence, options). Replan on evidence as it arrives. Never wait for full quiescence.\\n- Retry by mode: cap-hit or oom, respawn with smaller scope. Network-drop, retry as-is. Tool-error, retry on a different model. Unknown, retry once. Two retries, then abandon the unit and replan around it. Missing required SHA or method evidence uses the stricter assignment limit of one inspected retry. A changed scope or input revision gets a new assignment ID linked to its predecessor. Never retry denied permissions or unknown external outcomes into success.\\n- A zombie that returns hours late reconciles against the current frontier and ledger before anything is accepted. Salvage unique findings through a fresh unit, never a blind merge.\\n- When continued spawning would produce garbage tree-wide (bad upstream output, broken acceptance, dead infra), write a stop line at the top of the standing orders, let in-flight work finish, fix the cause, clear it.\\n- Bound your own infra retries the same way you bound a child's. After a few consecutive tool aborts, stop retrying. Write a terminal handoff to durable state (what is done, where it lives, the exact command to resume) and end the run.\\n- After a host restart, inspect worker state. Do not infer that local agents died or remote work survived. Re-read the standing orders and `units.tsv`, recompute the frontier, reattach surviving remote work by inspected PR and branch plus immutable assignment identity rather than stale agent id, respawn one sub-coordinator per track from its stored brief plus current state, drain, resume. A dead holder does not authorize takeover. `orch` rejects a stale lock until ownership review. Use `--force` only after explicit reviewed takeover permission; it can also steal a live lock.\\n\\n#### Escalation\\n\\nReaches the human, batched into the status page rather than per item: irreversible actions (force-push to shared branches, deploys, deletions, closing someone else's PR), genuine product or preference calls no experiment settles, a standing order that contradicts observed reality, a program-level dead end that survived a replan. Park each as a `gates.md` entry before asking, and route work around it.\\n\\nNever reaches the human: frontier nudges, restack mechanics, retries, CI flake triage, review-thread triage, format fixes, scope the brief already forbids (refuse and continue), and \\\"should I keep going\\\". Act and log within existing authorization. Park any missing permission instead of assuming it.\\n\\nMid-run discoveries fix only what blocks the frontier. Everything else parks in follow-ups. At this fan-out a small scope leak multiplies into PRs nobody asked for.\\n\\n**Reply:** at checkpoints and close: the predicate and the count against it from `units.tsv` and `ledger.tsv`, tracks and what each landed, the frontier (PR list plus SHAs), verdicts summary, what was abandoned and why, gates awaiting the human (the only asks), the store path, and the trail path. Numbers from the tables, not narrative. Include PR links.\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"
    },

    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/units-and-proof/outputs/raw/binding-1.json",
      "bytes": 3383,
      "sha256": "4bfe70104687ab37bf778b604ae804ae497a3819a689ffed9d6c50d86754fe92",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-units-and-proof",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\n  \"arguments\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/model-roles.md'\",\n    \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/units-and-proof\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 12000\n  },\n  \"return\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"a2079e\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000005458,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 718,\n    \"output\": \"# Model selection for portable roles\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](host-capabilities.md) before assigning a role. These [poteto](agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and [comment reviewer](agent-prompts/comment-sicko.md) prompts are instructions supplied to an exposed worker operation. Reading them does not register a custom agent or authorize delegation.\\n\\n| Role | Default requested model | Observed model | Supported effort | Fallback disclosure |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| poteto-agent | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\n| comment-sicko | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\n\\nFor each assignment, record requested model and effort, observed model and effort, supported effort values and their source, worker identity, and fallback disclosure. Use `unknown` for information the operation does not expose. `inherit-parent` records a selection policy, not an observed model name. Do not silently convert a requested model into an actual model.\\n\\nSelect an explicit model only from current exposed tool documentation or observed enumeration results. If enumeration is missing, disclose that limit and retain `inherit-parent`. Documentation examples are not a list of available models. Use only an effort supported for the selected model by the current operation; do not carry an incompatible inherited effort into an explicit selection.\\n\\nIf a selected model or effort is rejected, retain the rejection. Use an authorized inherited or supported alternative only after disclosing the changed selection. If the task requires that exact selection, stop and record blocked. A fallback never proves the original selection succeeded.\\n\\nIdentical observed models mean no model diversity. Different roles can supply distinct instructions on the same model. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified, even when requested model names differ. Different observed models support a model-diversity statement only for those observed assignments. Worker independence still requires successful spawn and result inspection.\\n\\nWhen workers are unavailable, apply an authorized role sequentially and label reduced capability. Preserve scope and permission boundaries. Do not claim independent review, background execution, or full worker support from sequential role switching.\\n\\nThe cached documentation from https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents describes inheritance and optional local agent configuration. It is context only. This package writes no local custom-agent configuration and claims no current host model or worker support from that documentation.\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"

    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/units-and-proof/outputs/raw/binding-0.json",
      "bytes": 7717,
      "sha256": "99137b082392e49692a95a6e80d650fc7dca8488abb0394a62b5ee722db29a64",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-units-and-proof",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\n  \"arguments\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/host-capabilities.md'\",\n    \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/units-and-proof\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 12000\n  },\n  \"return\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"3cac31\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000005583,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 1789,\n    \"output\": \"# Discover capabilities in the current session\\n\\nRead the tools exposed to this session before assigning work. Cached official-subagents.txt is documentation context; it proves no operation succeeded. Local agent configuration is not portable plugin configuration.\\n\\nRecord each binding as `{operation, tool, exposed, succeeded, evidence, limits}`. `tool` is the exact exposed identifier or null when absent. `succeeded` is true only after observing a result. Keep failed and unavailable bindings. Successful bindings become `successfulOperations` in host evidence, with a root-relative evidence file. The operation labels below describe purposes, never callable APIs.\\n\\n| Operation | Discovery and observation required |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| spawn | Find an exposed independent-worker operation; retain returned worker identity and exact assigned revision. |\\n| follow-up | Find the operation that sends input to that worker; observe acceptance by the same identity. |\\n| wait | Find the operation that waits for worker state; retain terminal or interrupted state. |\\n| inspect-result | Read the worker result and its referenced artifacts; a wait notification alone is insufficient. |\\n| stop | Observe cancellation or termination through the exposed operation. |\\n| read | Observe the requested file content through the available file tool. |\\n| isolated-write | Observe a write within the worker's assigned path and permissions. |\\n| history | Observe available prior turns or records; declare gaps explicitly. |\\n| execution | Observe command execution with exit status and output. |\\n| persistence | Write and read an authorized durable record; a claimed save is insufficient. |\\n| external-write | Record explicit authorization and action identity; inspect the result before replay. |\\n| resolve-ownership | Observe distinct owned paths before contested writes. |\\n| inspect-terminal | Observe the worker's terminal state after stop. |\\n| quarantine-artifacts | Observe partial artifacts withheld from integration. |\\n| inspect-prior-action | Read the interrupted action identity and prior outcome. |\\n| inspect-destination | Observe the destination state for that action identity. |\\n| safe-recovery | Observe explicitly authorized idempotent recovery after identity and destination inspection. |\\n\\nNo default API names are assigned here. Tool names differ by host and session. An exposed tool may still be denied or fail. Record requested and actual models only when observed; otherwise use `unknown`. A requested model never proves the actual model.\\n\\nA denied or unavailable operation blocks the branch that requires it. Sequential substitution for required independent work is reduced capability and uses `reduced-pass`; ordinary direct completion can fully pass. Missing persistence limits session pickup even when current work succeeds. Preserve user authorization and host permission boundaries through every fallback.\\n\\nWorker passes require observed spawn and result inspection. Each registry entry declares `requiredObservations` for `pass` and `reduced-pass`; tool observations need exposed bindings and existing evidence artifacts; native text observations use the separate reviewed native record below. Full cancellation requires stop, terminal inspection and artifact quarantine. Full persistence requires durable write and readback. Full interrupted-action recovery requires prior identity inspection, destination inspection, safe recovery, explicit external permission and a recorded prior action identity even when no external-write observation is listed.\\n\\nPermission checks follow performed operations. Isolated writes and persistence require granted writes. External writes and safe recovery require granted external permission and prior identity. Authorized read-only reductions may retain denied writes; their limits must preserve denied or unavailable permissions. Required observations remain applicable to those reductions.\\n\\nHost evidence is stored as object or array JSON files under `docs/parity/evidence/`. Required fields are defined by `docs/parity/evidence.schema.json`. Artifacts and operation evidence must be existing files inside the tested root. Test fixtures belong in `tests/` and prove validator behavior only.\\n\\nRun `python3 tools/validate_evidence.py --root . --commit <tested-package-commit>` through Bun, for example `bun -e 'const p=Bun.spawnSync([\\\"python3\\\",\\\"-B\\\",\\\"tools/validate_evidence.py\\\",\\\"--root\\\",\\\".\\\",\\\"--commit\\\",process.argv[1]],{env:{...process.env,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE:\\\"1\\\"}});process.stdout.write(p.stdout);process.stderr.write(p.stderr);process.exit(p.exitCode)' <tested-package-commit>`. It reads every JSON record below the evidence directory, prints each error, and exits 1 for invalid records or 0 for valid records. An empty directory has no invalid records and supplies no behavioral evidence.\\n\\nThe required registry file in `docs/parity/case-registry.json` declares applicability. Missing or non-file registry, unknown case, structural-only entry or unlisted host rejects behavioral validation. Maintenance fixtures use a temporary copy of this registry; they cannot bypass applicability. Required hosts must receive observed evidence or explicit blocked records before release claims. Structural checks have no required host and cannot populate host status. Later producers propose task-owned entries; only the coordinator integrates them. Source paths select callers through the task 3 case contract. Case definitions alone leave host statuses `not-run`.\\n\\n## Direct completion and recorded observations\\n\\n`direct` means the task is completed in the current context. Restatement, supplied-text writing and complete installed discovery can pass directly. `sequential` is reserved for replacing a required independent method with sequential work and remains reduced. A text response has no invented callable tool. Record supplied input, the actual response and qualitative outcome review as `nativeObservations`, each with its existing listed evidence artifact and named reviewer. Their labels are `supplied-input`, `response` and `review-outcome`. The reviewer assesses the case's expected outcome; validation checks evidence relationships and does not decide prose quality.\\n\\nEach registry entry declares allowed execution modes and required permissions for each status. A grant is required for the performed action: worker spawn, command execution and stop need execution; isolated output or persistence needs writes; external actions need their separate permission and prior identity. Read-only discovery does not inherit an execution grant requirement. Tool observations still name an actually exposed tool and existing evidence. Native observations cannot claim tool discovery, worker identity or saved writes.\\n\\nFull Arena and Swarm results additionally provide `inputRevision` and a listed `methodReceipt` artifact. An exposed `inspect-method` operation must reference that receipt. Assignment operations name `assignmentId` and the corresponding inspected output. See the [structured method contract](orchestration-contract.md). All positive maintenance fixtures are synthetic; none is an observed host result.\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"
    },

      "path": "fixtures/cases/units-and-proof/outputs/raw/helper-0.json",
      "bytes": 2378,
      "sha256": "0c811870e75ca65b7bcba35e8825ba5e9247aecb909f0065eb1e069003a8416b",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-units-and-proof",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\n  \"arguments\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/show-me-your-work/scripts/log.sh'\",\n    \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/units-and-proof\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 10000\n  },\n  \"return\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"7e922b\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000005542,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 430,\n    \"output\": \"#!/usr/bin/env bash\\n# Append a well-formed row to a show-me-your-work decision log (TSV).\\n# Usage: log.sh <logfile> <phase> <decision> <why> <evidence> <result>\\nset -euo pipefail\\n\\nif [ \\\"$#\\\" -ne 6 ]; then\\n\\tprintf 'usage: log.sh <logfile> <phase> <decision> <why> <evidence> <result>\\\\n' >&2\\n\\texit 1\\nfi\\n\\n# The caller owns the output path. Validate every argument before any write.\\nif [ -z \\\"$1\\\" ]; then\\n    printf 'error: an explicit owned output path is required\\\\n' >&2\\n    exit 1\\nfi\\nfor value in \\\"$@\\\"; do\\n    case \\\"$value\\\" in\\n        *$'\\\\t'*|*$'\\\\n'*|*$'\\\\r'*)\\n            printf 'error: tabs, newlines and carriage returns are not allowed\\\\n' >&2\\n            exit 1\\n            ;;\\n    esac\\ndone\\nlogfile=\\\"$1\\\"\\nshift\\n\\nlogdir=\\\"$(dirname \\\"$logfile\\\")\\\"\\nif [ -n \\\"$logdir\\\" ] && [ \\\"$logdir\\\" != \\\".\\\" ] && [ ! -d \\\"$logdir\\\" ]; then\\n\\tmkdir -p \\\"$logdir\\\"\\nfi\\n\\n# Use `>>` here, never `>`. A network mount can fail this test for a log\\n# that exists. Then the cost is one stray header line, not the rows.\\nif [ ! -s \\\"$logfile\\\" ]; then\\n\\tprintf 'ts\\\\tphase\\\\tdecision\\\\twhy\\\\tevidence\\\\tresult\\\\n' >> \\\"$logfile\\\"\\nfi\\n\\nts=\\\"$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)\\\"\\n# Record separators were rejected above. Prefix any cell\\n# whose first char a spreadsheet would parse as a formula (=, +, -, @)\\n# with a single quote. The skill expects this log to be read in\\n# spreadsheets, so attacker-controlled evidence (PR titles, filenames,\\n# generated text) must not become formula execution when a reviewer\\n# opens the file.\\nclean() {\\n\\tlocal v\\n\\tv=\\\"$1\\\"\\n\\tcase \\\"$v\\\" in\\n\\t\\t=*|+*|-*|@*) printf \\\"'%s\\\" \\\"$v\\\" ;;\\n\\t\\t*) printf '%s' \\\"$v\\\" ;;\\n\\tesac\\n}\\nprintf '%s\\\\t%s\\\\t%s\\\\t%s\\\\t%s\\\\t%s\\\\n' \\\\\\n\\t\\\"$ts\\\" \\\"$(clean \\\"$1\\\")\\\" \\\"$(clean \\\"$2\\\")\\\" \\\"$(clean \\\"$3\\\")\\\" \\\"$(clean \\\"$4\\\")\\\" \\\"$(clean \\\"$5\\\")\\\" \\\\\\n\\t>> \\\"$logfile\\\"\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"

    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/units-and-proof/outputs/raw/source-06.json",
      "bytes": 11323,
      "sha256": "83b6aeebdfe9a951bdadc5293de76e294edf7a0f7df893843372f619b54f02a2",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-units-and-proof",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\n  \"arguments\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/autopilot-stack.md'\",\n    \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/units-and-proof\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 20000\n  },\n  \"return\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"c7e0a3\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000006458,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 2694,\n    \"output\": \"### Autopilot-stack\\n\\nYou own the stack, never the landing. Build and verify the queue within authorized scope, then hand the operator one linear base-branch stack to review and land. This is the sibling of Autopilot-full. Use for \\\"autopilot-stack\\\", \\\"stack them, don't ship\\\", or \\\"build the stack, I'll land it\\\".\\n\\nRead [poteto-mode](../SKILL.md), [Autopilot-full](autopilot-full.md), [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), the [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md), [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md), and the [poteto role prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) in full. Inherit the parent model unless explicit selection is supported. Actual models remain unknown until observed. Discover execution, forge, independent workers, history, persistence, wake and stop bindings. Upstream autonomy grants no permission. Commits, pushes, PR creation, retargets, verdict posts and other external writes require actual authorization and observed outcomes. Never install or schedule implicitly.\\n\\nRead each complete adapted dependency before its step. The [Bugbot rubric](../references/bugbot-triage.md), [Babysit](babysit.md), [Shipping](shipping.md), and [Opening a PR](opening-a-pr.md) may be pending. A missing contract blocks its dependent operation. A canonical-source read during implementation does not supply an executable adapted contract. Continue only independent authorized work and report the exact gap.\\n\\n1. Run the owner loop unchanged except for this playbook's topology and landing rules. Resolve the forge once for the program. GitHub CLI (`gh`) is the default. If `command -v origin` succeeds and Origin can resolve the repository, use `origin pr ...` for PR operations. Otherwise stay on `gh` and record the fallback. Never require Graphite (`gt`). Assign one independent worker per PR under the full poteto role prompt and assignment contract. The owner builds, makes the first authorized push, opens a ready PR before self-proof, runs gates and CI with receipts, triages Bugbot skeptically, completes the discovered `deslop` companion or an authorized replacement review, runs [/no-comments](../../no-comments/SKILL.md), and babysits to green. Report a missing companion as an unmet gate, never silently waive it. Owners parallelize self-contained work with disjoint files and one writer per branch. Within about 15 minutes of actual dispatch, every authorized owner starts an uncommitted `decisions.tsv` via [show-me-your-work](../../show-me-your-work/SKILL.md), pushes its first snapshot and opens the PR ready, never draft. Push again after every verifiable unit with hooks on; a WIP commit is fine. Keep and return the trail. Keep `children.tsv` as in Autopilot-full step 2, with child identity, expected runtime and state. Missing runtime history stays unknown, not an invented timeout. Retain that step's first-rebase, fix-round base and committed-head pre-review checks, with the root performing topology writes here.\\n2. Audit on a real loop. On the operator's go, register an exposed, authorized hourly wake with a prompt for this tick. The source alias `/loop 1h` is not a portable scheduler binding. Observe registration identity, cadence and stop operation before claiming it active. At each tick, re-read this playbook from trunk using `git show <observed-trunk-ref>:<actual-repository-relative-playbook-path>`, record both values and verify the read. Audit against it and fix drift. Probe every owner with a generic liveness or status check. Count only commits, pushes, PR or check deltas and store reports as progress. Stand down and replace a lane that errors or exceeds an observed expected runtime without side effects, without waiting for a polite return. Apply Autopilot-full step 6's agent-list and children checks, repeat replacement checks, collect trails, and finish the tick only when no delegated work remains. If stop cannot be observed, issue zero-writes orders, record unverified termination, inspect reachable state and quarantine partial artifacts. Do not dispatch a competing writer until ownership is safe. Missing persistent wake permits useful authorized current-turn ticks and a saved continuation brief as reduced capability; it never promises hourly work after the turn.\\n3. Hold the operator gates. State-then-wait applies. A request to state the plan is not a go. On the operator's stop, every owner takes an immediate zero-writes hold and retains its brief until released.\\n4. Verify each round. The owner reports its code-ready head SHA once the shipped code is final, and `STACK-READY` with the exact head SHA when self-proof, CI and babysit are green. Apply Autopilot-full step 4 in full with STACK-READY in place of merge-ready. Audit receipts, run independent [swarm](../../swarm/SKILL.md) lanes at that exact revision, re-run gates, prove load-bearing behavior on the real artifact per [prove-it-works](../../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md), audit the diff with at least two focused lanes, and run the same regression scenario on current trunk. If trunk lacks the feature, record that and verify the added behavior and user end state. A live lane is mandatory. Send every proven finding, including notes, in one fix-forward with red tests covering all defect sites or a repro receipt. Add the defect to the next brief. Every patch-changing push starts a fresh round except results valid under Shipping's patch-id rule. Nothing enters the stack unverified.\\n5. Append on a clean verdict, never ship. No owner merges, arms auto-merge, or closes a PR. The root also stops at delivery and never merges, closes a PR, or arms merge-when-ready under this playbook. Append a clean PR to one linear base-branch stack in verified order or the operator's specified order. Publication of each verdict in its PR body or comment remains an explicitly authorized write.\\n6. Keep one topology writer and parallel build writers. Owners push only their branches and report the tip, current base and intended parent. Coordinate a zero-writes hold on the affected owner branch before root topology work. The root alone fetches the intended parent, verifies its exact tip, rebases the child onto that tip, checks `ls-remote` and publishes with `--force-with-lease` only within authorization. Never force-push a shared branch. Follow Opening a PR's built-in PR tool contract for creation, edits, retargets and readiness when that tool is exposed. The following CLI examples apply only when it is not. Create a ready PR with `origin pr create --status open --base <parent-branch>` or `gh pr create --base <parent-branch>`. Retarget with `origin pr edit <pr> --base <parent-branch>` or `gh pr edit <pr> --base <parent-branch>`. Only the root PR targets trunk. Never submit or register the chain through `gt`. Read back each resulting head and base before accepting the topology.\\n7. Absorb drift at the root and re-verify what moved. Fetch current trunk and rebase bottom-to-top. Conflicts in an owner's files go to that owner for its own slice; the root pushes the result under the same exclusive topology ownership. Each rewritten SHA above the rebase voids the old verdict until Shipping's full patch-id rule validates its retained lanes. Record verdict head, base and stable base-to-head patch-id. Changed patches return to step 4. Tests, docs or lint-only differences use Shipping's two builds at the verdict SHA and one at current head, with each difference and noise explained. A dev-server lane or other lane without build output must rerun. Even an unchanged patch-id requires fresh mergeability and CI after every rewritten push. Preserve Autopilot-full's countersign rule: a genuinely new pinned gate or budget raise stops for a fresh root countersign after verifier proof, recorded under the approval contract and checked by a lane. Never bypass forge approval. Absorbing landed values is drift, not a raise.\\n8. Deliver one linear chain of verified PRs, reviewable bottom-up in the resolved forge. Every link carries its current verifier verdict in the body or comment when posting is authorized. If posting is denied, return local verdicts as reduced capability and do not claim forge delivery. The operator reviews and lands the chain with their own clicks or by arming merge-when-ready. A later authorized request to land routes to Shipping as a distinct workflow and requires its fresh checks.\\n\\nBefore resuming and before another write, read back the continuation brief, decisions and children trails, assignment attempts, countersigns and operator gates. Resolve every PR's current head, base, intended parent, verdict head/base/patch-id, checks and mergeability against the live destination or authorized fixture. Reconcile changes with steps 4 and 7. Inspect each prior interrupted action's stable identity, destination and observed outcome, including pushes, creates, retargets, verdict posts and wake registration. Completed actions are not replayed. Unknown outcomes block replay; safe recovery requires explicit authorization, destination inspection and an appropriate idempotency mechanism. A saved head or completion notification never proves current state. Missing history or inaccessible destinations leave affected writes blocked.\\n\\nWhen independent owners or verifier lanes are unavailable, authorized sequential work is reduced capability and cannot satisfy the clean independent verdict gate. Missing real-artifact control leaves the live lane blocked. Keep unknown models and absent cross-model trail review explicit. Preserve show-me-your-work's Attention section and audit only this run's accessible transcript. Use its reviewed [log helper](../../show-me-your-work/scripts/log.sh) and [template](../../show-me-your-work/references/decision-log-template.tsv) with explicit owned local paths. Save and read back an authorized continuation brief with the bottom-to-top queue, branch ownership, exact heads/bases/parents, verdict revisions and patch-ids, pending actions and outcomes, trail paths, countersigns, gates, missing capabilities and next checks. If persistence is unavailable, return it inline and say no durable save occurred. Neither form proves unattended recovery or automatic pickup.\\n\\nChoose Autopilot-full for independent PRs when landing authority is granted. Choose Autopilot-stack for sequenced or coupled work, operator review before landing, or withheld merge authority.\\n\\nReply with links to the stack root and tip, a one-line current verdict summary per link, and anything parked or excluded with its reason. Include open gates, capability gaps, trail and continuation paths, and Attention when a trail was produced. Do not fabricate PR links or describe a local proposed chain as a published stack.\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"
