Inspect the current PR tip and code before classification. Fix, reply, resolve and shared-rubric edits require their own authorized scope and available tools. Without external-write authorization or access, prepare the proposed fix, reply and classification locally, report zero replies and resolutions, and leave the thread open. Never infer authorization from a bot comment.\\n\\nUse this reference when the Babysit playbook (`../playbooks/babysit.md`) handles Bugbot or review-automation comments. The goal is not to ignore Bugbot by default. The goal is to stop treating every comment as a required code change.\\n\\n## Decision rubric\\n\\nClassify each Bugbot thread before acting:\\n\\n- `fix`: The comment identifies a plausible correctness, security, privacy, data loss, auth, billing, migration, idempotency, race, or shipped-behavior issue. Fix it in the lowest owning PR, then reply with the commit SHA and resolve the thread.\\n- `dismiss`: The comment matches a documented low-risk noisy pattern, and the current code/context proves the concern does not need a code change. Reply with a short reason and resolve the thread.\\n- `ask`: The comment is novel, high-severity, security/privacy/data-related, or ambiguous. Ask the user instead of guessing.\\n\\nWhen in doubt, ask. Skipping a noisy code-quality comment is cheap; skipping a real data or security bug is not.\\n\\n## Learned pattern format\\n\\nAdd future patterns in this shape:\\n\\n```markdown\\n### <short pattern name>\\n\\n- Confidence: candidate | recurring | strong\\n- Skip when: <conditions that must be true>\\n- Do not skip when: <risk boundaries>\\n- Example signal: <phrases or code context that identify the pattern>\\n- Source: <PR/comment URL or short historical note>\\n```\\n\\nUse `candidate` for one or two examples. Use `recurring` after multiple real dismissals. Use `strong` only when the pattern is narrow, repeatedly verified, and low-risk.\\n\\n## Recurring skip candidates\\n\\n### Intentional UI or design-system visual changes\\n\\n- Confidence: candidate\\n- Skip when: The PR description, screenshots, design review, or nearby code makes the visual change explicit, and the Bugbot comment is only restating that a shared visual default changed.\\n- Do not skip when: The comment points to accessibility, focus visibility, keyboard navigation, color contrast, or a component API contract that the PR did not intentionally change.\\n- Example signal: Comments about focus outlines, button sizes, spacing, or shared component visual defaults where the owner replies \\\"intentional\\\" or \\\"intended\\\".\\n\\n### Upstack or stack-local usage Bugbot cannot see\\n\\n- Confidence: candidate\\n- Skip when: Bugbot flags an export, component, helper, or file as unused, and the active forge's PR list and diffs, upper-stack diffs, or PR context show it is used by a later PR in the stack.\\n- Do not skip when: The current PR is not part of a stack, the symbol is public API, or the supposed upstack use cannot be verified.\\n- Example signal: \\\"Exported component is never used\\\" with a human reply like \\\"used upstack\\\".\\n\\n### Temporary duplication during parallel implementation\\n\\n- Confidence: candidate\\n- Skip when: The PR intentionally duplicates a small amount of code to keep a new path parallel to an old path that is being deleted, replaced, or proven out.\\n- Do not skip when: The duplicated code changes security, billing, data access, API behavior, or a long-lived shared abstraction would clearly reduce risk.\\n- Example signal: \\\"Significant duplication\\\" or \\\"duplicated validation logic\\\" where the owner explains the old path will be deleted or the duplicate logic is intentionally local.\\n\\n### Existing framework or component invariant covers the warning\\n\\n- Confidence: candidate\\n- Skip when: The concern is already guaranteed by a shared component, framework contract, type invariant, or single source of truth visible in the current diff or nearby code.\\n- Do not skip when: The invariant is assumed but not enforced, depends on timing, or crosses async/state boundaries where values can diverge.\\n- Example signal: Comments about missing max-height on an inner popover when the shared popover enforces viewport bounds, or nullable values where the local checked value and passed value share the same source.\\n\\n### Owner-declared follow-up or deferred cleanup\\n\\n- Confidence: candidate\\n- Skip when: The PR owner explicitly says the issue is a known follow-up, the behavior is not made worse by the current PR, and the comment is not about a high-risk area.\\n- Do not skip when: The agent is acting without owner input, the issue is medium/high severity product behavior, or deferring would merge a new regression.\\n- Example signal: \\\"I'll worry about that later\\\" or \\\"we'll delete this eventually\\\".\\n\\n### Self-withdrawn or explicit false-positive rule comments\\n\\n- Confidence: recurring\\n- Skip when: The comment body or a later Bugbot reply explicitly says the finding is withdrawn, compliant, or a false positive, and the agent can verify the relevant rule locally.\\n- Do not skip when: The only evidence is a human saying \\\"false positive\\\" on a high-risk issue without explanation.\\n- Example signal: A file-naming rule comment whose body says the file is already compliant.\\n\\n## Ask by default\\n\\nDo not auto-skip these categories, even if a previous PR dismissed something similar:\\n\\n- Security, privacy, auth, billing, data retention, training-data, and permission-boundary findings.\\n- High-severity findings.\\n- Migration, schema, idempotency, concurrency, and cross-system behavior findings.\\n- Comments where the suggested fix is small and clearly reduces risk without changing product intent.\\n\\nHistorical data showed humans sometimes dismiss security/data-flow comments. Treat those as owner judgment calls, not team-wide skip rules.\\n\\n## Candidate learnings from recent babysits\\n\\nAppend new candidate learnings here during or after babysitting when they look team-useful but not yet mature. Prefer promoting recurring candidates into the section above once several PRs confirm the pattern.\\n\\n### Manual reimplementations of native browser behavior\\n\\n- Confidence: candidate\\n- Skip when: Practically never. When a diff replaces native browser behavior with a manual equivalent (native sticky → JS-positioned clones, native scroll targeting → forwarded wheel/touch events, paint-order occlusion → masks/clip-path), Bugbot's logic-bug findings against that code have been consistently legitimate.\\n- Do not skip when: The finding concerns event-forwarding gaps (wheel deltaMode, touch pans, scroll-chaining at edges, tap slop), mask/clip hit-testing divergence, or observer-vs-React state timing races in such code. Default to fix.\\n- Example signal: \\\"masks do not affect hit-testing\\\", \\\"overlay blocks wheel scroll\\\", \\\"ignores deltaMode\\\", \\\"runs in the IntersectionObserver callback before React applies state\\\".\\n- Source: one sticky-occlusion PR: six Bugbot passes, roughly eighteen findings, every one fixed rather than dismissed.\\n\\n### Contract-test drift claims are cheaply verifiable — run the test first\\n\\n- Confidence: candidate\\n- Skip when: Never skip the verification itself; it costs one command. When a PR\\n  ships a contract test that pins protocol or documentation prose (regexes over\\n  a SKILL.md, snapshot of doc wording), and Bugbot claims \\\"the test no longer\\n  matches the doc\\\" (or vice versa), run that test on the PR tip before\\n  classifying. A red run confirms the claim empirically; a green run is a\\n  concrete disproof for the dismissal reply.\\n- Do not skip when: n/a — this is a verification shortcut, not a dismissal\\n  pattern. Note that repeat-pass lean-dismiss heuristics would misfire here:\\n  prose-pinning tests drift precisely BECAUSE earlier fix rounds edit the prose.\\n- Example signal: \\\"Contract test omits the pre-fix wait\\\" on a PR whose earlier\\n  fix commits reworded the pinned passage; the test run on the tip failed on\\n  exactly the cited assertion.\\n- Source: one prose-pinning PR with eight Bugbot passes; the claim was real on\\n  pass 7 despite every earlier pass being fixed-and-resolved.\\n\\n### Stale security-review finding already fixed later in the same PR\\n\\n- Confidence: candidate\\n- Skip when: An agentic security review (or similar) claims a missing authz/validation call, and the current PR tip clearly includes that exact gate (with tests), typically added in a later hardening commit after the review ran.\\n- Do not skip when: The cited helper is a no-op for the principal under discussion, the check runs after the side effect it guards, or coverage for the claimed principal is missing.\\n- Example signal: A HIGH \\\"missing authorization check\\\" finding while the exact guard is already called before the side effect on the tip.\\n- Source: one webhook-endpoint PR whose hardening commit postdated the review run.\\n\\n### Widening a deliberately narrow error condition would mask the real error\\n\\n- Confidence: candidate\\n- Skip when: The finding asks to broaden a narrow error condition (a specific\\n  `errno`, error code, or status class) into a catch-all, and that narrowness\\n  encodes a real distinction. The canonical shape is a dependency fallback\\n  gated on `ENOENT`: \\\"binary is not installed\\\" is a different situation from\\n  \\\"the command ran and failed\\\". Retrying on any non-zero exit would re-run a\\n  legitimate failure (not found, expired auth, network) against the fallback\\n  and then report the fallback's error, hiding the true one.\\n- Do not skip when: The narrow condition misses a case in the SAME category\\n  (another \\\"binary unusable\\\" errno such as `EACCES`, another transport-level\\n  failure), the unhandled path loses data or leaves partial state, or the retry\\n  is idempotent AND the original error is still surfaced.\\n- Example signal: \\\"only retries when X fails with ENOENT … never tries the\\n  fallback even when a working Y exists\\\", pointing at code whose fallback\\n  exists for a missing dependency rather than a failed operation.\\n- Source: one CLI-rename PR whose fallback existed for a missing binary rather\\n  than a failed command.\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"

      "text": "{\n  \"arguments\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/show-me-your-work/SKILL.md'\",\n    \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/units-and-proof\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 20000\n  },\n  \"return\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"3405ee\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000005792,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 2114,\n    \"output\": \"---\\nname: show-me-your-work\\ndescription: \\\"Keep a reviewable decision trail for long-running or unattended work: a TSV log with one row per decision (what, why, evidence, result). Local by default; commit it when a reviewer needs the trail to trust the result. Use for /show-me-your-work, autonomous or multi-phase runs, or work a human reviews after stepping away.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Show me your work\\n\\nKeep one canonical log.\\n\\n## The format\\n\\nA single TSV file, one row per decision. Cells stay single-line. Evidence is a pointer, not prose.\\n\\nCopy [references/decision-log-template.tsv](references/decision-log-template.tsv) (the header row) to start a clean log. Columns:\\n\\n- **ts.** ISO8601 timestamp.\\n- **phase.** The phase or workstream.\\n- **decision.** What was chosen or done, one line.\\n- **why.** The reason in plain words. If a principle drove it, say it plainly, not as a jargon tag.\\n- **evidence.** A link or path that proves it: commit SHA, PR number, `file:line`, or an artifact, trace, or screenshot path. Never a paragraph.\\n- **result.** The outcome or predicate state: `tests green`, `reverted`, `pixel-diff 0`, `INCONCLUSIVE`, `open`.\\n\\nAn example, plain-spoken so a reviewer reads it at a glance.\\n\\n```\\nts\\tphase\\tdecision\\twhy\\tevidence\\tresult\\n2026-05-24T09:02:00Z\\tframe\\tcounted the work first, about 100 components and roughly 75 hours\\twanted to know the size before starting a long run\\tcommit 3a9f1c2\\tfound 5 things to sort out before starting\\n2026-05-24T09:40:00Z\\tharness\\ttook screenshots of the old version before changing anything\\tso we can compare old against new and catch any visual change\\tscripts/snapshot.sh, baseline/\\tsaved 120 reference screenshots\\n2026-05-24T11:15:00Z\\twidget\\tmoved the widget styles over without changing how it looks\\tkeep the change small and the result identical\\tcommit 7c21e0a, pixel-diff 0\\tlooks identical, tests pass\\n2026-05-24T12:30:00Z\\twidget\\tthrew out a helper's work because its screenshots were blank\\tchecked the real files instead of trusting its summary\\tworktree reset\\treverted, tightened the instructions for next time\\n```\\n\\n## Logging a row\\n\\nWrite each entry the way you'd tell a teammate what you did. Plain words, concrete actions, no AI speak or abstract jargon (the **unslop** skill applies to log text too).\\n\\nUse the helper [scripts/log.sh](scripts/log.sh) with `<logfile> <phase> <decision> <why> <evidence> <result>` through an authorized execution binding. Pass an explicit nonempty local output path that this task owns. The helper rejects tabs, newlines and carriage returns in any argument before creating directories or writing bytes. It stamps `ts`, writes the header on first use, preserves previous rows, and prefixes any cell starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, or `@` with a single quote. Use this helper for generated or user-supplied cells so validation and formula protection apply consistently. Host execution still requires an authorized binding.\\n\\nLog decision points and checkpoints, not every action: a fork chosen, a unit completed with its verification result, a pivot or revert with its trigger, a blocker surfaced, a gate fixed. For loop runs, one row per iteration. Skip the trivial and self-evident.\\n\\nA run is one agent conversation, including its later turns and any summary of it. A pickup, a replacement agent, or a new chat starts a new run. When a run adds to a log that already has rows, its first row has phase `start`, and so does its first row after another run's `start` row. So a run that comes back to a log in a later turn first reads the log's last rows to see whether another run wrote since. A `start` row names the `ts` range of the rows before it that this run did not write, and its evidence names this run, such as its agent id. Use phase `start` for nothing else.\\n\\n## Where it lives\\n\\nBy default the log is a working artifact, not committed. Keep it at `decisions.tsv` in the work dir, or `.audit/<task-slug>.tsv` when several efforts run at once, and leave it out of git.\\n\\nCommit it only when the work is ambitious enough that a reviewer needs the trail to trust the result.\\n\\n## Rules\\n\\n- Append-only. A wrong call gets a new row that supersedes it. Never edit or delete history.\\n- Prefer evidence produced by committed scripts over hand-made one-offs (the **encode-lessons-in-structure** principle skill).\\n\\n## Audit the log against the transcript\\n\\nAt the end of the run, before handing back, check the log told the truth. Discover this run's transcript through the current session's history binding, or an explicitly identified transcript in the active workspace. The source used `agent-transcripts/`; do not assume the host provides that directory. Never search unrelated private chats or global transcript directories. If this run's transcript is inaccessible or incomplete, record the gap and do not claim a complete transcript audit. Walk this run's rows against what actually happened. Each stretch of them begins at one of this run's `start` rows, or at the first row if this run created the log, and ends at the next `start` row of another run:\\n\\n- Check that every row maps to a real decision or action.\\n- Check that each row's evidence resolves and shows what the row claims.\\n- A fork, pivot, or abandoned approach that shaped the work but isn't logged is a gap. Add it.\\n\\nCorrect the log, not the story. The audit never edits or removes a row, even an invented one. When a row records neither a real decision nor a real action, or its claim or evidence is wrong, add a row that supersedes it with what actually happened and a pointer that resolves. This audit does not check rows outside this run's stretches. If this run's own work shows one of them is wrong, supersede it like any wrong call.\\n\\n## Cross-model review of the trail\\n\\nBefore handing back, use an authorized exposed independent-worker binding to request a reviewer on a different model family from the one that did the work. Follow [model selection](../../references/model-roles.md) and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Observe the worker identity, result and actual model family before claiming independent cross-model review. If no authorized worker or verified different family is available, record that review as blocked. An authorized sequential scan is reduced capability and does not satisfy this review requirement. Self-review is not a substitute. The subagent reads the audit trail and the run's transcript, then flags what the user should pay attention to. Not a redo of the work, a scan for what's suboptimal or risky.\\n\\n- Decisions logged with weak or absent evidence.\\n- Verification steps skipped or claimed without proof in the transcript.\\n- Choices that look risky in hindsight (premature, scope-creeping, papering over a symptom).\\n- Gaps the user would otherwise miss on a casual skim.\\n\\nEvery reply for a run that produced a trail ends with an \\\"Attention\\\" section. Lead with the reviewer's model on its own line (`reviewed by <model>`), then list each flag pointing to specific rows or moments. \\\"No flags\\\" is a valid value. The model name is not.\\n\\n## Reviewing the trail\\n\\nRead top to bottom, follow the evidence pointers, spot-check. GitHub renders a committed TSV as a table. `column -s$'\\\\t' -t decisions.tsv` renders it in a terminal.\\n\\n## Composing this skill\\n\\nOther skills route their audit trail here instead of inventing one. Reference it by name and let it own the format. Don't restate the columns.\\n\\n## Host capabilities and safety\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) for history, execution, persistence and reviewer bindings. Write only to an authorized local path, then read back the saved trail before claiming persistence. A log is evidence of recorded decisions, not proof of unattended execution or recovery. Do not include credentials, tokens, raw secret-bearing output or unrelated private data. Use a safe evidence pointer or a redacted local artifact instead. Record only observed results; unresolved predicates stay `open` or `INCONCLUSIVE`.\\n\\nThe default local trail does not authorize a commit. Commit, publish, message or other external action requires explicit authorization. Preserve append-only history and scope every transcript audit to this run. When review is blocked, keep the Attention section and state `reviewed by unknown` if no actual reviewer model was observed, followed by the capability gap. Never use `No flags` to imply an unperformed review.\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"

Encode the rule as a lint, metadata flag, runtime check, or script instead of more text.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Encode Lessons in Structure\\n\\nEncode recurring fixes in mechanisms (tools, code, metadata, automation) instead of textual instructions. Every error, human correction, and unexpected outcome is a learning signal. Capture it, route it, and close the loop.\\n\\n**Why:** Textual instructions are easy to miss. They require the reader to notice, remember, and comply. Structural mechanisms (lint rules, metadata flags, runtime checks, automation scripts) enforce the rule without cooperation.\\n\\n**Pattern:**\\nWhen you catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time:\\n1. Ask: can this be a lint rule, a metadata flag, a runtime check, or a script?\\n2. If yes, encode it. Delete the instruction\\n3. If no (requires judgment), make the instruction more prominent and add an example of the failure mode\\n\\n**Pick the strongest mechanism.** When more than one mechanism would work, choose the strongest the situation allows (an unrepresentable state that cannot compile, then a lint or banned API that fails CI, then a canonical helper, then a runtime check), because agents copy whatever the surrounding code already does and a weaker guard becomes the next template.\\n\\n**Corollary:** If the fix is structural, only use the structural fix. The instruction is the symptom.\\n\\n**Feedback loop:**\\n- **Capture every correction.** When the human intervenes or tests fail, decide if it's a one-off or a pattern.\\n- **Route to the right layer.** One-off -> brain note. Recurring fix -> skill or lint rule. Systemic issue -> principle.\\n- **Close the loop.** Don't just record. Apply now or create a concrete todo.\\n\\n**Anti-patterns:**\\n- Acknowledging without recording (\\\"I'll keep that in mind\\\" does not persist)\\n- Recording without routing (a brain note about a lint rule that should exist is wasted unless the lint rule gets implemented)\\n- Fixing without generalizing (fixing one instance while leaving the recurring pattern intact)\\n\\n## Session capabilities and authorization\\n\\nFollow [session capability discovery](../../references/host-capabilities.md) for reads, writes, execution and durable persistence. Apply mechanisms within the authorized task. This principle does not authorize installation, global settings or external actions. Treat a brain note as an authorized durable record when persistence is available; claim it saved only after write and readback. If persistence is unavailable, state that limit and include the concrete todo in the current response.\\n\\nIf writes or execution are unavailable, provide a reduced proposal that names the check, its integration point and the remaining implementation or verification work. If the rule or source needed to select a mechanism is missing, identify the missing inputs and keep the proposal provisional. Delete a redundant instruction only once the structural fix enforces its rule. Preserve judgment-dependent guidance with a prominent failure example.\\n\\nPreserve explicit-invocation metadata without assuming the host enforces it. Claim enforcement, implementation or verification only for observed operations.\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"

Execution starts on the operator's explicit go, under the execution playbook the plan names.\\n\\n**Verification.** Tests alone are not sufficient verification.       "text": "{\n  \"arguments\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md'\",\n    \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/units-and-proof\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 20000\n  },\n  \"return\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"55f18f\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000005375,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 612,\n    \"output\": \"---\\nname: principle-sequence-verifiable-units\\ndescription: \\\"Apply to multi-step work (sweeps, migrations, runs of similar edits) and to how you stack commits and PRs. Break work into small units that each end in a verifiable state, check each before the next, and order delivery so the sequence proves itself to a reviewer.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Sequence work into verifiable units\\n\\nOrder work as a sequence of small units, each ending in a state you can check, and don't advance until the current one is green.\\n\\n**Why:** A break caught at the unit that caused it is cheap to localize. A break caught after a batch is buried, and you have already built further on a broken base. Sequencing those same units into a delivery a reviewer can replay turns \\\"trust me\\\" into \\\"watch it go red, then green.\\\"\\n\\n**Execution.** In a sweep, migration, or any run of similar edits, verify each change before starting the next. Each unit is a before/after bracket: known-good state, one change, run the check, then proceed. Rebase onto clean trunk first so every check measures against the real baseline. When a lever does the edits, the per-unit check is nearly free. Run it anyway.\\n\\n**Delivery.** Stack commits and PRs in the order that proves the work. The canonical shape is the failing test first, then the fix on top. Other story orders are a subtraction before the reshape, a baseline capture before the treatment, the scaffold before the feature. Each commit lands on its own and the sequence reads as an argument.\\n\\nThe sequencing complement to the **prove-it-works** principle skill, which keeps each check real, and the **build-the-lever** principle skill, which makes the per-unit check cheap.\\n\\n## Capability and authorization boundary\\n\\nUse [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to establish available inspection and execution, and the [orchestration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md) for write ownership and authorization. Rebase onto clean trunk only when authorized and safe for the current work. Commits and PRs also require authorization and an available operation. If a required check cannot run, state that it remains unverified and block advancement; an unrun check is never green. A reduced workflow may provide the ordered units, specific checks and delivery plan while identifying unperformed operations. Preserve `disable-model-invocation` metadata without assuming host enforcement.\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"

      "text": "{\n  \"arguments\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"bun --version\",\n    \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/units-and-proof\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 1000\n  },\n  \"return\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"8d4d21\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000005708,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 2,\n    \"output\": \"1.4.2\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"

    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/units-and-proof/outputs/raw/source-20.json",
      "bytes": 6485,
      "sha256": "f3aad3834f0b859a94449b2ade6e8c80b2eb59105e659d86cb1a5185f5377bd5",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-units-and-proof",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\n  \"arguments\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/interrogate/SKILL.md'\",\n    \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/units-and-proof\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 20000\n  },\n  \"return\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"03b612\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000005833,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 1464,\n    \"output\": \"---\\nname: interrogate\\ndescription: \\\"Use for \\\\\\\"interrogate\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"adversarial review\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"multi-model review\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"challenge this\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"stress test this code\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"find blind spots\\\\\\\", or \\\\\\\"tear this apart\\\\\\\". Multiple LLM reviewers challenge changes from independent angles.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Interrogate\\n\\nSpawn one reviewer per configured model to adversarially review code changes. Each model gets the same prompt and rubric. The adversarial signal comes from model diversity, not assigned personas.\\n\\nThe deliverable is a synthesized verdict. Do NOT auto-apply changes.\\n\\n## Step 1, Determine Scope\\n\\nIdentify what to review from context:\\n\\n- If the user points at specific files or a diff, use that\\n- If on a feature branch, run `git diff main...HEAD` (or the appropriate base branch) for the full changeset\\n- If the user's message references recent work, gather the relevant files\\n\\nPackage the diff (or file contents) plus any surrounding context files the reviewers need to understand the code.\\n\\n## Step 2, State the Intent\\n\\nBefore spawning reviewers, state the intent explicitly. Derive this from:\\n\\n- The user's message\\n- Commit messages\\n- PR description if one exists\\n- The code itself\\n\\nWrite one clear paragraph. If you're unsure about the intent, ask the user before proceeding.\\n\\n## Step 3, Spawn Reviewers\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model selection](../../references/model-roles.md), and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Launch authorized independent read-only reviewers together through the exposed worker operation, one per explicitly configured reviewer selection. Extend or shrink Reviewer A/B labels to the selection count. Give each an immutable assignment, the same input revision and separate result ownership. Inspect actual results before synthesis.\\n\\nThe canonical source used a local model rule and default requests from the Claude and Grok families. The exact historical defaults are preserved as provenance in the [adaptation record](../../docs/parity/adaptations/interrogate.json). These historical requests do not establish available models. Preserve explicit session selections. With no configured selections, request two inherited reviewers and disclose that inherited or identical models do not establish model diversity. For auto or inherit-parent, omit explicit model selection. Never treat aliases as rejected model names.\\n\\nSelect explicit models and effort only from current exposed tool support. If a configured request is rejected, disclose it and use an authorized supported alternative, preferring the same family and reasoning tier where available. If the exact selection is required, report blocked. Record requested and observed models separately; missing observations remain unknown. Do not open a PR or change settings to repair upstream defaults without explicit authorization.\\n\\nWhen independent workers are unavailable, apply the same prompt and rubric sequentially with authorization and label reduced capability. Do not call repeated passes independent reviewers or consensus. Missing observed model diversity limits that claim even when worker independence succeeds. Denied review or missing source blocks grounded findings. Do not install tools or configure agents as an incidental step.\\n\\nRead `references/reviewer-prompt.md` and fill in the template with:\\n1. The stated intent\\n2. The diff or file contents\\n3. The review rubric from `references/rubric.md`\\n4. The code-quality lens from `references/code-quality-review.md`\\n\\nThe same filled template goes to all reviewers, so every model applies the code-quality lens.\\n\\n## Step 4, Synthesize\\n\\nAs results come back, build a unified picture:\\n\\n1. **Parse all findings** from the reviewers\\n2. **Identify consensus**. Findings raised by 2+ models independently are highest signal.\\n3. **Identify lone-model findings**. Still worth reading, but weight accordingly.\\n4. **Deduplicate**. Different models may describe the same issue differently. Merge these and note which models raised it.\\n5. **Note disagreements**. If one model flags something and another explicitly says the opposite, that's useful context for the verdict.\\n\\n## Step 5, Lead Judgment\\n\\nYou are the lead reviewer, a pragmatic senior engineer, not a neutral aggregator.\\n\\nRead `references/lead-judgment.md` for the full framework.\\n\\nCategorize every finding using these buckets:\\n\\n- **Act on**. Real issues affecting correctness, security, or maintainability given the actual goals. These would block a real PR.\\n- **Consider**. Legitimate points, but you're not sure they outweigh the cost of addressing them right now. Worth the user's attention.\\n- **Noted**. Technically valid but not actionable. Context-dependent, premature optimization, or low-impact given the current stage.\\n- **Dismissed**. Wrong, nitpicky, or missing context. Brief explanation why.\\n\\nFor each finding, include:\\n- Which model(s) raised it\\n- The category (act on / consider / noted / dismissed)\\n- A one-line rationale for the categorization\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nPresent the verdict in this structure:\\n\\n### Intent\\n> [The stated intent paragraph from Step 2]\\n\\n### Reviewers\\nReport requested and observed models, worker independence, model diversity and capability limits. Missing observations remain unknown.\\n\\n- Reviewer [label]: [model name], [N findings] (one bullet per reviewer)\\n\\n### Act On\\n[Findings that should be addressed. For each: description, which models raised it, why it matters.]\\n\\n### Consider\\n[Findings worth thinking about. For each: description, which models raised it, tradeoff involved.]\\n\\n### Noted\\n[Valid but low-priority. Brief list.]\\n\\n### Dismissed\\n[Rejected findings with brief rationale.]\\n\\n### Agreement Map\\n[Where did models agree, where did they diverge, and what does the pattern of agreement/disagreement tell us?]\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"
    },

    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/units-and-proof/outputs/raw/source-19.json",
      "bytes": 4663,
      "sha256": "b60444bf486b05291e7a6dea360fe171e9d7a5ac7a9bbe4c6fdd1c9f1a1fb5a9",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-units-and-proof",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\n  \"arguments\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/how/SKILL.md'\",\n    \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/units-and-proof\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 20000\n  },\n  \"return\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"691f74\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000005458,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 1023,\n    \"output\": \"---\\nname: how\\ndescription: \\\"Use for \\\\\\\"how does X work\\\\\\\", code walkthroughs before changing something, and placement / ownership / layering questions (\\\\\\\"where should this live\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"which package owns this\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"is this the right layer\\\\\\\"). Explains subsystem architecture, runtime flow, onboarding mental models. Use why for motivation.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# How\\n\\nExplore the codebase to answer \\\"how does X work?\\\" questions. Produce architectural explanations at the level of a senior engineer onboarding onto a subsystem, enough to build a working mental model, not so much that it reads like annotated source code.\\n\\nBefore dispatch, read [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Bind worker operations to tools actually exposed and authorized in this session. Request the inherited parent model and effort unless a supported explicit selection is observed. Retain rejections and disclose replacements; an unavailable required selection is blocked. Record requested and observed selections separately, using unknown for unobserved values.\\n\\nKeep explorers and explainers read-only. Worker assignments record their role, distinct angle, exact input revision, identity and inspected results under the assignment contract. Reading a prompt does not register a worker. If independent workers are unavailable or unauthorized, disclose reduced capability and perform the same exploration and explanation roles sequentially. Never claim parallel or independent exploration from sequential work. If source access is unavailable or denied, report the gap and limit the explanation to inspected evidence.\\n\\n## Step 1. Assess Complexity\\n\\nIf the scope is ambiguous, state your interpretation and explore. The user can redirect.\\n\\n- **Simple** (a single module, a small utility, a narrow question such as \\\"how does function X work\\\"): no explorers. One explainer explores and explains in a single pass. Go to Step 2b.\\n- **Complex** (a subsystem spanning multiple files or services, a cross-cutting feature, a full architectural overview): use parallel explorers when available, then hand off to the explainer. Go to Step 2a.\\n\\nWhen in doubt, take the simple path.\\n\\n## Step 2a. Explore (complex questions only)\\n\\nDecompose the question into 2 to 4 exploration angles, each a distinct slice of the subsystem. Dispatch all explorers together through an observed worker binding, with distinct assignments:\\n\\n- Role: how explorer\\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\\n- Access: read-only\\n\\nEach explorer gets the prompt in `references/explorer-prompt.md` with its angle filled in. If workers are unavailable, explore those distinct angles sequentially and retain separate findings. Then go to Step 3.\\n\\n## Step 2b. Direct Explain (simple questions)\\n\\nAssign one explainer worker that explores and explains in one pass:\\n\\n- Role: how explainer\\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\\n- Access: read-only\\n\\nBuild its prompt from `references/explainer-prompt.md` without the explorer-findings section. The explainer reads and traces the source itself. If workers are unavailable, apply this role sequentially. Go to Step 4.\\n\\n## Step 3. Synthesize (complex questions only)\\n\\nOnce all explorer results have been inspected, assign one explainer worker to synthesize their findings into one explanation:\\n\\n- Role: how explainer\\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\\n- Access: read-only\\n\\nBuild its prompt from `references/explainer-prompt.md` with every explorer's findings filled in. In reduced capability, synthesize the sequential findings under the same read-only role.\\n\\n## Step 4. Present\\n\\nPresent the explainer's output to the user. Light edits for clarity or context from the conversation are fine. Do not substantially rewrite it.\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nThe explanation uses the sections defined in `references/explainer-prompt.md`, dropping any that do not apply: Overview, Key Concepts, How It Works, Where Things Live, Gotchas.\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"
    },

Full verification requires unit evidence, live evidence, and performance evidence or justified non-applicability.\\\";\\nconst SUB_BLOCKS = [\\n\\t\\\"Depends on.\\\",\\n\\t\\\"Files.\\\",\\n\\t\\\"Build.\\\",\\n\\t\\\"You see.\\\",\\n\\t\\\"Verify, unit.\\\",\\n\\t\\\"Verify, live.\\\",\\n\\t\\\"Verify, perf.\\\",\\n\\t\\\"Review gate.\\\",\\n\\t\\\"Merge.\\\",\\n];\\nconst PROGRAM_H3 = [\\\"Arm the program\\\", \\\"Spawn owners\\\", \\\"PR mechanics\\\", \\\"Verdict and merge\\\", \\\"Boot recipe\\\"];\\nconst PROGRAM_MARKERS = [\\\"baseline\\\", \\\"status message\\\"];\\nconst HOW_TO_READ_MARKERS = [\\n\\t\\\"One box is one unit of work\\\",\\n\\t\\\"names the evidence\\\",\\n\\t\\\"Check a box only when its evidence exists\\\",\\n\\t\\\"playbooks/\\\",\\n\\tRULE,\\n];\\nconst PERF_ITEMS = [\\\"Metric.\\\", \\\"Probe.\\\", \\\"Baseline.\\\", \\\"Rule.\\\"];\\nconst BOX = /^\\\\s*- \\\\[[ x]\\\\] (.*)$/;\\n\\nconst file = process.argv[2];\\nif (!file || process.argv.length !== 3) {\\n\\tconsole.error(\\\"Usage: bun --no-install check-plan.mjs <pstack-plan.md>\\\");\\n\\tprocess.exit(2);\\n}\\n\\nlet raw;\\ntry { raw = fs.readFileSync(file, \\\"utf8\\\").split(/\\\\r?\\\\n/); }\\ncatch (error) { console.error(`${file}: cannot read plan (${error.code})`); process.exit(2); }\\nconst problems = [];\\nconst fail = (line, message) => problems.push(`${file}:${line}: ${message}`);\\n\\nlet start = 0;\\nif (raw[0] === \\\"---\\\") {\\n\\tstart = raw.indexOf(\\\"---\\\", 1) + 1;\\n}\\n\\nconst lines = [];\\nlet fence = false;\\nfor (let i = start; i < raw.length; i++) {\\n\\tconst text = raw[i];\\n\\tconst n = i + 1;\\n\\tif (/^```/.test(text)) fence = !fence;\\n\\tlines.push({ n, text, code: fence });\\n\\tif (fence) continue;\\n\\tconst prose = text\\n\\t\\t.replace(/`[^`]*`/g, \\\"`\\\")\\n\\t\\t.replace(/!\\\\[[^\\\\]]*\\\\]\\\\([^)]*\\\\)/g, \\\"\\\")\\n\\t\\t.replace(/\\\\]\\\\([^)]*\\\\)/g, \\\"]\\\");\\n\\tif (/[\\\\u2013\\\\u2014]/.test(prose)) fail(n, \\\"long dash\\\");\\n\\tif (/[\\\\u2018\\\\u2019\\\\u201c\\\\u201d]/.test(prose)) fail(n, \\\"curly quote\\\");\\n\\tif (/: \\\\S/.test(prose)) fail(n, \\\"mid-sentence colon\\\");\\n}\\n\\nconst h2 = (l) => (!l.code && l.text.startsWith(\\\"## \\\") ? l.text.slice(3).trim() : null);\\nconst sections = [];\\nfor (const l of lines) {\\n\\tconst title = h2(l);\\n\\tif (title !== null) sections.push({ title, n: l.n, body: [] });\\n\\telse if (sections.length) sections.at(-1).body.push(l);\\n}\\nconst find = (title) => sections.find((s) => s.title === title);\\nconst bodyText = (s) => s.body.map((l) => l.text).join(\\\"\\\\n\\\");\\nconst boxes = (ls) => ls.filter((l) => !l.code && BOX.test(l.text)).map((l) => ({ n: l.n, text: l.text.match(BOX)[1] }));\\n// This is a pstack template validator. It never edits a plan or validates\\n// implementation plans owned by another workflow.\\nif (!find(\\\"How to read this\\\") || !find(\\\"Program checklist\\\")) {\\n\\tconsole.error(`${file}:1: not a pstack multi-phase plan; leave other workflow plans unchanged`);\\n\\tprocess.exit(1);\\n}\\nconst capabilities = find(\\\"Capabilities\\\");\\nconst capabilityText = capabilities ? bodyText(capabilities) : \\\"\\\";\\nconst declared = {};\\nfor (const name of [\\\"Independent workers\\\", \\\"Live control\\\", \\\"Scheduling\\\", \\\"Remote\\\", \\\"Model selection\\\"]) {\\n\\tconst line = capabilities?.body.find((l) => !l.code && l.text.startsWith(`${name} `));\\n\\tconst match = line?.text.match(/^(.*?) (available|unavailable)\\\\. Evidence `([^`<>]+)`\\\\./);\\n\\tif (!match) fail(line?.n ?? 1, `${name}: missing capability evidence and available/unavailable declaration`);\\n\\telse declared[name] = { available: match[2] === \\\"available\\\", text: line.text, n: line.n };\\n}\\nconst capacity = Number(declared[\\\"Independent workers\\\"]?.text.match(/Capacity (\\\\d+)\\\\./)?.[1] ?? 0);\\nif (declared[\\\"Independent workers\\\"]?.available && capacity < 1) fail(declared[\\\"Independent workers\\\"].n, \\\"available independent workers require observed capacity\\\");\\nfor (const [name, pattern] of [[\\\"Scheduling\\\", /\\\\/loop\\\\s|scheduled audit/i], [\\\"Remote\\\", /cloud VM|remote creation/i], [\\\"Model selection\\\", /Ten lanes on `|explicit model|grok-4/i]]) {\\n\\tfor (const line of lines) if (!line.code && pattern.test(line.text) && !declared[name]?.available) fail(line.n, `${name} claim lacks available capability evidence`);\\n}\\n\\nconst h1 = lines.findIndex((l) => !l.code && l.text.startsWith(\\\"# \\\"));\\nif (h1 === -1) fail(1, \\\"no H1 title\\\");\\nconst howToRead = find(\\\"How to read this\\\");\\nif (!howToRead) fail(1, 'no \\\"## How to read this\\\" is ${intro.length} lines, under ten required`);\\n\\tfor (const marker of HOW_TO_READ_MARKERS) {\\n\\t\\tif (!bodyText(howToRead).includes(marker)) fail(howToRead.n, `How to read this lacks \\\"${marker}\\\"`);\\n\\t}\\n}\\n\\nconst program = find(\\\"Program checklist\\\");\\nif (!program) fail(1, 'no \\\"## Program checklist\\\" section');\\nelse {\\n\\tconst h3s = program.body.filter((l) => !l.code && l.text.startsWith(\\\"### \\\")).map((l) => l.text.slice(4).trim());\\n\\tlet cursor = 0;\\n\\tfor (const name of PROGRAM_H3) {\\n\\t\\tconst at = h3s.findIndex((t, i) => i >= cursor && t.startsWith(name));\\n\\t\\tif (at === -1) fail(program.n, `Program checklist lacks \\\"### ${name}\\\" in order`);\\n\\t\\telse cursor = at + 1;\\n\\t}\\n\\tfor (const marker of PROGRAM_MARKERS) {\\n\\t\\tif (!bodyText(program).includes(marker)) fail(program.n, `Program checklist lacks \\\"${marker}\\\"`);\\n\\t}\\n}\\n\\nconst close = find(\\\"Close the program\\\");\\nif (!close) fail(1, 'no \\\"## Close the program\\\" section');\\nconst programIndex = sections.indexOf(program);\\nconst closeIndex = sections.indexOf(close);\\nconst prSections = programIndex === -1 || closeIndex === -1 ? [] : sections.slice(programIndex + 1, closeIndex);\\nif (prSections.length === 0) fail(1, \\\"no PR sections between Program checklist and Close the program\\\");\\n\\nconst report = [];\\nfor (const pr of prSections) {\\n\\tconst heads = [];\\n\\tfor (const l of pr.body) {\\n\\t\\tif (l.code) continue;\\n\\t\\tconst m = l.text.match(/^\\\\*\\\\*([^*]+)\\\\*\\\\*(.*)$/);\\n\\t\\tif (m && SUB_BLOCKS.includes(m[1])) heads.push({ name: m[1], n: l.n, rest: m[2].trim(), lines: [] });\\n\\t\\telse if (heads.length) heads.at(-1).lines.push(l);\\n\\t}\\n\\tconst names = heads.map((h) => h.name);\\n\\tif (names.join(\\\"|\\\") !== SUB_BLOCKS.join(\\\"|\\\")) {\\n\\t\\tfail(pr.n, `${pr.title}: sub-blocks are [${names.join(\\\", \\\")}], expected [${SUB_BLOCKS.join(\\\", \\\")}]`);\\n\\t}\\n\\tconst block = (name) => heads.find((h) => h.name === name);\\n\\tconst counts = {};\\n\\tfor (const h of heads) counts[h.name] = boxes(h.lines).length;\\n\\n\\tconst depends = block(\\\"Depends on.\\\");\\n\\tif (depends && depends.rest === \\\"\\\") fail(depends.n, `${pr.title}: Depends on names nothing`);\\n\\tfor (const name of [\\\"Files.\\\", \\\"Build.\\\", \\\"You see.\\\", \\\"Verify, unit.\\\", \\\"Merge.\\\"]) {\\n\\t\\tconst b = block(name);\\n\\t\\tif (b && boxes(b.lines).length === 0) fail(b.n, `${pr.title}: ${name} has no box`);\\n\\t}\\n\\tfor (const name of [\\\"Verify, unit.\\\", \\\"Verify, live.\\\", \\\"Verify, perf.\\\"]) {\\n\\t\\tconst b = block(name);\\n\\t\\tif (b && !b.rest.startsWith(RULE)) fail(b.n, `${pr.title}: ${name} does not open with the rule`);\\n\\t}\\n\\n\\tconst live = block(\\\"Verify, live.\\\");\\n\\tif (live) {\\n\\t\\tconst status = live.rest.match(/Status (full|reduced|blocked)\\\\./)?.[1];\\n\\t\\tconst laneCount = Number(live.rest.match(/Lanes (\\\\d+)\\\\./)?.[1] ?? -1);\\n\\t\\tif (!status) fail(live.n, `${pr.title}: live requires Status full, reduced, or blocked`);\\n\\t\\tif (status === \\\"full\\\" && (!declared[\\\"Live control\\\"]?.available || !declared[\\\"Independent workers\\\"]?.available)) fail(live.n, `${pr.title}: full live verification requires control and independent workers`);\\n\\t\\tif (status === \\\"full\\\" && laneCount > capacity) fail(live.n, `${pr.title}: live lane count exceeds observed worker capacity`);\\n\\t\\tif ((status === \\\"reduced\\\" || status === \\\"blocked\\\") && !/Reason .+\\\\. Evidence `([^`<>]+)`\\\\./);\\n\\t\\t\\t\\tif (!receipt) fail(lane.n, `${pr.title}: checked live requires observed head and evidence receipt, a checkbox is not proof`);\\n\\t\\t\\t\\telse {\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t\\tconst artifact = path.resolve(path.dirname(path.resolve(file)), receipt[2]);\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t\\ttry { if (!fs.statSync(artifact).isFile() || fs.statSync(artifact).size === 0) throw new Error(\\\"empty\\\"); }\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t\\tcatch { fail(lane.n, `${pr.title}: checked live evidence receipt is missing or empty`); }\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t}\\n\\t\\t\\t}\\n\\t\\t}\\n\\t\\tif (status === \\\"blocked\\\" && live.lines.some((l) => /^\\\\s*- \\\\[x\\\\]/.test(l.text))) fail(live.n, `${pr.title}: blocked live cannot have checked success boxes`);\\n\\t\\tif (laneCount > 0 && !/Regression.*trunk.*head/i.test(lanes[0]?.text ?? \\\"\\\")) fail(live.n, `${pr.title}: first lane must name regression against trunk and head`);\\n\\t}\\n\\n\\tconst perf = block(\\\"Verify, perf.\\\");\\n\\tif (perf) {\\n\\t\\tconst text = perf.lines.map((l) => l.text).join(\\\"\\\\n\\\");\\n\\t\\tconst items = boxes(perf.lines).map((b) => b.text.split(\\\" \\\")[0]);\\n\\t\\tif (/Not applicable\\\\./.test(text)) {\\n\\t\\t\\tif (!/Not applicable\\\\. Reason .+\\\\. Evidence `[^`<>]+`\\\\./.test(text) || items.length) fail(perf.n, `${pr.title}: performance non-applicability needs separate reason and evidence, without success boxes`);\\n\\t\\t} else if (items.join(\\\"|\\\") !== PERF_ITEMS.join(\\\"|\\\")) fail(perf.n, `${pr.title}: perf boxes are [${items.join(\\\", \\\")}], expected [${PERF_ITEMS.join(\\\", \\\")}]`);\\n\\t}\\n\\n\\tconst gate = block(\\\"Review gate.\\\");\\n\\tif (gate) {\\n\\t\\tconst gateBoxes = boxes(gate.lines);\\n\\t\\tconst applicable = /(?:^|\\\\. )Interaction applicable\\\\./.test(gate.rest);\\n\\t\\tconst notApplicable = /(?:^|\\\\. )Interaction not applicable\\\\. Reason .+\\\\. Evidence `[^`<>]+`\\\\./.test(gate.rest);\\n\\t\\tif (applicable === notApplicable) fail(gate.n, `${pr.title}: explicit interaction applicability required, with reason and evidence for non-applicability`);\\n\\t\\tif (!declared[\\\"Independent workers\\\"]?.available && !/Reduced review\\\\. Reason .+\\\\. Evidence `[^`<>]+`\\\\./.test(gate.rest)) fail(gate.n, `${pr.title}: unavailable workers require explicit reduced review`);\\n\\t\\tif (declared[\\\"Independent workers\\\"]?.available && (!/Independent review available\\\\./.test(gate.rest) || !/Independent reviewer/.test(gate.lines.map((l) => l.text).join(\\\"\\\\n\\\")))) fail(gate.n, `${pr.title}: independent review required when workers available`);\\n\\t\\tif (gate.rest.startsWith(\\\"None.\\\")) {\\n\\t\\t\\tif (gateBoxes.length) fail(gate.n, `${pr.title}: Review gate says None but has boxes`);\\n\\t\\t\\tif (applicable) fail(gate.n, `${pr.title}: applicable interaction cannot omit operator screenshot and video review`);\\n\\t\\t} else {\\n\\t\\t\\tconst text = gate.lines.map((l) => l.text).join(\\\"\\\\n\\\");\\n\\t\\t\\tif (gateBoxes.length === 0) fail(gate.n, `${pr.title}: Review gate has no box`);\\n\\t\\t\\tif (!/Save `[^`<>]+`/.test(text)) fail(gate.n, `${pr.title}: Review gate names no evidence artifact`);\\n\\t\\t\\tif (applicable) for (const word of [\\\"screenshot\\\", \\\"video\\\", \\\"operator\\\"]) if (!text.includes(word)) fail(gate.n, `${pr.title}: Review gate lacks \\\"${word}\\\"`);\\n\\t\\t}\\n\\t}\\n\\n\\tconst total = boxes(pr.body).length;\\n\\tconst cells = SUB_BLOCKS.filter((s) => s !== \\\"Depends on.\\\").map((s) => `${s.replace(/[ ,.]+/g, \\\"-\\\").replace(/-$/, \\\"\\\").toLowerCase()}=${counts[s] ?? 0}`);\\n\\treport.push(`${pr.title}  boxes=${total}  ${cells.join(\\\" \\\")}`);\\n}\\n\\nif (closeIndex !== -1) {\\n\\tconst tail = sections.slice(closeIndex + 1);\\n\\tfor (const s of tail) {\\n\\t\\tif (!s.title.startsWith(\\\"Appendix\\\")) fail(s.n, `\\\"## ${s.title}\\\" after Close the program is not an appendix`);\\n\\t}\\n\\tif (!tail.some((s) => s.title.includes(\\\"Prototype evidence\\\"))) fail(close.n, 'no \\\"## Appendix ... Prototype evidence\\\" section');\\n}\\n\\nfor (const line of report) console.log(line);\\nconsole.log(`${prSections.length} PR sections, ${problems.length} problems`);\\nfor (const p of problems) console.error(p);\\nprocess.exit(problems.length ? 1 : 0);\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"

      "sha256": "e5f024b430504c5db4212f551205ba1678299a2156f73fd805a217be6a69eb9e",

      "text": "{\n  \"arguments\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-guard-the-context-window/SKILL.md'\",\n    \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/units-and-proof\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 20000\n  },\n  \"return\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"45120d\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.0000055,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 497,\n    \"output\": \"---\\nname: principle-guard-the-context-window\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when context is filling up: large outputs, long files, repeated reads, fan-out planning. Route bulk to subagents; keep summaries in the main thread, not raw payloads.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Guard the Context Window\\n\\nThe context window is finite and non-renewable within a session. Every token should be worth its cost.\\n\\n**Why:** Context overflow degrades reasoning quality, creates compression artifacts, and halts progress.\\n\\n**Pattern:**\\n- **Isolate large payloads.** Route verbose outputs, screenshots, and large documents to subagents. The main context gets summaries, not raw data.\\n- **Keep frequently used content inline.** Templates and references used on every invocation belong in the skill file, not in separate files that cost a read each time.\\n- **Size phases and cap scope.** Limit files per phase, set turn budgets, account for mechanism costs.\\n\\n## Capability and permission boundaries\\n\\nUse the [session capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to check evidence access and independent-worker operations. Delegate only when the task authorizes it and the session exposes those operations. Follow the [assignment and integration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md), retain observed worker identities and inspect their results before reporting summaries. This principle grants no additional permissions.\\n\\nIf independent workers are unavailable or unauthorized, narrow accessible evidence into bounded sequential phases. Keep summaries in the main thread and label this reduced capability. Sequential work does not establish worker independence. If required evidence is inaccessible, name the missing input and access limit; keep the plan provisional and do not claim to have read or summarized it.\\n\\nPreserve the invocation metadata above without assuming the host enforces it. Report delegation and inspection only after observing the authorized operations.\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"
    },

    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/units-and-proof/outputs/raw/source-14.json",
      "bytes": 2872,
      "sha256": "f3b594b5a3dc6095d276c3224c88b15cb83ddffdf6496e882e6c5f576c41d550",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-units-and-proof",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\n  \"arguments\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-never-block-on-the-human/SKILL.md'\",\n    \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/units-and-proof\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 20000\n  },\n  \"return\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"bac8d3\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000005875,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 580,\n    \"output\": \"---\\nname: principle-never-block-on-the-human\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when tempted to ask 'should I do X?' on reversible work. Proceed, present the result, let the human course-correct after the fact; reserve confirmation for irreversible actions.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Never Block on the Human\\n\\nThe human supervises asynchronously. Agents must stay unblocked. Make reasonable decisions, proceed, and let the human course-correct after the fact.\\n\\n**Why:** Every permission pause stalls the pipeline and makes the human the bottleneck. Since code changes are reversible and reviewable, a wrong decision usually costs less than blocking.\\n\\n**Pattern:**\\n- **Proceed, then present.** Do the work, show the result. Don't ask \\\"should I do X?\\\" Do X, explain why.\\n- **Make the system self-healing.** When you notice a problem, log it and fix it in the next round.\\n\\n**Boundaries:**\\n- **Irreversible actions** (force-push, delete production data, send external messages) still require confirmation.\\n- **Reversible actions** (write code, edit notes, split tasks) should proceed without blocking.\\n- **Product direction** comes from the human. *Execution* should not block.\\n\\n## Authorization and session capabilities\\n\\nApply the proceed-then-present pattern to authorized work within the assigned scope. Reversibility does not grant permission. An explicit user review gate still controls the gated action; prepare its reviewable result and continue independent authorized work while awaiting review. Human product direction remains authoritative.\\n\\nUse the capabilities actually available in the current session, following [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md). If writes are denied or unavailable, provide a reviewable proposed change and say it was not applied. Continue work that available permissions support. If no permitted action can advance the task, report the missing capability or input. Do not claim an edit, save or execution succeeded without observing it.\\n\\nKeep problem logging and next-round fixes within authorized scope and available persistence. Preserve confirmation for irreversible actions, and any explicit user or host approval requirement, including external messages.\\n\\nThe source invocation metadata is retained. Do not assume every host enforces `disable-model-invocation`.\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"
    },
